<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every complete connected noncompact Kähler manifold with strictly positive holomorphic bisectional curvature is biholomorphic to complex Euclidean space. This resolves Yau's uniformization conjecture positively in every complex dimension.

- **claim-uniformization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-uniformization)): Let $M$ be a connected noncompact complex manifold of complex dimension $n\geq1$. If $M$ admits a smooth complete Kähler metric with strictly positive holomorphic bisectional curvature, then $M$ is biholomorphic to $\mathbb C^n$.
  - Explanation: A smooth complete Kähler metric with strictly positive holomorphic bisectional curvature determines the complex manifold up to biholomorphism with complex Euclidean space. The conclusion concerns the complex structure rather than flatness of the given metric.
- **claim-initial-analytic-data** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-initial-data)): The following objects and estimates are available.* 1. *There is a smooth strictly plurisubharmonic function $\psi$ with $\psi\le C(1+r)$.* 2. *Holomorphic functions and holomorphic tangent fields interpolate arbitrary finite jets at a finite set of points. There are finitely many holomorphic functions defining an injective immersion $F:M\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F6}% \longrightarrow% \EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}% }\mathbb C^N$, and finitely many global holomorphic tangent fields spanning $T^{1,0}M$ at every point.* 3. *At every point $x$ there is a global holomorphic canonical section $S$ with $S(x)\ne0$ and $\log\|S\|_g^2\le C_S(1+r)$. There is also a smooth function $\Xi$ such that $$\label{prelim:xi} \partial\bar\partial\Xi\ge\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(g),\qquad \Xi\le C(1+r^{3/2}).$$* 4. *There is a smooth exhaustion $u\ge1$ such that $$\label{prelim:exhaustion} C^{-1}(1+r)\le u\le C(1+r),\qquad \|\partial u\|_g\le C,\qquad \|\partial\bar\partial u\|_g\le C/u.$$* 5. *If $S(o)\ne0$ is chosen as in <span class="upright">(iii)</span>, then, for $L\ge1$, $$\label{prelim:averages} \frac{1}{\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}B(o,L)}\int_{B(o,L)} \bigl\|\log\|S\|_g^2\bigr\|\,dV_g\le CL, \qquad \frac{1}{\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}B(o,L)}\int_{B(o,L)}R_g\,dV_g\le C/L.$$* *Neither $F$ nor $\psi$ is asserted to be proper. The exhaustion in <span class="upright">(iv)</span> has a two-sided complex Hessian bound.
  - Explanation: The initial geometry supplies a strictly plurisubharmonic function, finite holomorphic interpolation and spanning families, canonical sections, a Ricci potential, a controlled exhaustion, and averaged curvature estimates. Properness is not asserted for the immersion or the strictly plurisubharmonic function.
- **claim-hodge-heat-order** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-hodge-heat)): For bounded real $(1,1)$-forms, Hodge heat is well defined by cutoff limits of the complete $L^2$ semigroup. It preserves order and obeys $$-Cg\le A\le Cg\quad\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F9}% \Longrightarrow% \EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}% }\quad -Cg\le\mathcal H_aA\le Cg, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_g\mathcal H_aA=H_a\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_g A.$$ If $A$ is also smooth and closed, then $$\label{prelim:hodge-identity} \mathcal H_aA =A+\partial\bar\partial\int_0^aH_b\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_g A\,db.$$ If a Lipschitz function $f$ is constant off a compact set and $\partial\bar\partial f\le Cg$ as currents, then $$\label{prelim:hessian-preservation} \partial\bar\partial H_af\le Cg\qquad(a>0).$$
  - Explanation: Complete Hodge heat preserves Hermitian order and commutes with scalar heat after tracing. Closed initial forms admit a scalar potential formula, and compactly supported variations of Lipschitz functions retain their upper complex Hessian bound after heat smoothing.
- **claim-global-localized-flow** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-localized-flow)): There is a smooth Kähler–Ricci flow $h_t$ on $M$, for $0\le t<\infty$, with $h_0=g$. There are smooth real functions $U,P$ and $\rho$ such that $$\label{flow:identities} \begin{gathered} 0<h_t\le g,\qquad h_t=g-t\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(g)+\partial\bar\partial U,\qquad U(\cdot,0)=0,\\ P=U_t=\log\frac{\det h_t}{\det g},\qquad \rho=-P\ge0,\qquad R_{h_t}=-P_t\ge0. \end{gathered}$$ For each finite $T$, there is $C_T$ such that $$\label{flow:rho-average} \sup_{0\le t\le T} \frac{1}{\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_gB_g(o,L)}\int_{B_g(o,L)}\rho(x,t)\,dV_g(x) \le C_TL\qquad(L\ge1).$$ At every fixed $x$, $\rho(x,t)\le C_x(1+t)$ for all $t\ge0$. Furthermore, on each finite time interval there is a smooth positive function $Q$ and a constant $c>0$ with $$\label{flow:proper-supercaloric} Q\ge c(1+r^2),\qquad (\partial_t-\Delta_{h_t})Q\ge c\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{h_t}g.$$ Smoothness at $t=0$ means smoothness with all one-sided time derivatives.
  - Explanation: Complete Hermitian approximations yield a global smooth Kähler–Ricci flow, local potential identities, averaged logarithmic volume-loss control, and proper supercaloric barriers. Positive-time completeness is established later.
- **claim-integrable-comparison-cutoff** (construction, [source](#stmt-flow-cutoff-lemma)): For every sufficiently large fixed integer $k$, there is a positive function $\chi(x,t)$ on $M\times[0,T]$, smooth in space and continuously differentiable in time one-sided at the initial surface, such that $$\label{flow:cutoff-bounds} \begin{gathered} C_T^{-1}u^{-k}\le\chi\le C_Tu^{-k},\qquad \|\partial\chi\|_g\le C_T\chi/u,\\ \partial\bar\partial\chi\le C_T\chi h_t, \qquad \partial_t\chi\le C_T(1+t^{\beta-1})\chi \quad(t>0). \end{gathered}$$
  - Explanation: An evolving correction to a polynomial spatial weight controls its complex Hessian by the evolving metric. Its time derivative has an integrable coefficient at the initial surface.
- **claim-scalar-comparisons** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-scalar-comparison)): Fix a finite $T>0$. The following conclusions hold for the flow of Theorem <a href="#thm:localized-flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:localized-flow">4</a>.* 1. *The function $\rho$ is the minimal nonnegative solution with zero initial values of $$\label{flow:rho-source} (\partial_t-\Delta_{h_t})\rho =\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{h_t}\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(g).$$ There are nonnegative smooth supercaloric functions $\rho_j$ on $M\times[0,T]$ such that $$\label{flow:rho-tails} 0\le\rho_j\le\rho,\qquad \rho_j\ge\rho-C_j,\qquad \rho_j\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F6}% \longrightarrow% \EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}% }0 \text{ locally uniformly on }M\times[0,T].$$ Each fixed $\rho_j$ has all spatial and one-sided time derivatives smooth at $t=0$ and bounded on compact spacetime sets.* 2. *Suppose $v$ is upper semicontinuous, bounded on compact spacetime sets, $0\le v\le\rho$, and satisfies, in the upper viscosity sense on $0<t<T$, $$\label{flow:nonlinear-comparison} \partial_tv\le e^{-v}\Delta_{h_t}v+(1-e^{-v})R_{h_t}.$$ Then $v=0$ on $M\times[0,T)$.* *Neither assertion requires completeness of $h_t$.
  - Explanation: The logarithmic volume loss is the minimal nonnegative zero-data potential of the Ricci trace source and admits smooth exterior-source tails. The stated nonlinear viscosity inequality forces a locally bounded nonnegative function dominated by that volume loss to vanish, before completeness is available.
- **claim-smooth-scalar-majorant** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-majorant)): Fix $T>0$ and let $v$ be upper semicontinuous, bounded on compact spacetime sets, and satisfy $0\le v\le\rho$ on $M\times[0,T]$. Suppose that $v$ satisfies <a href="#flow:nonlinear-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:nonlinear-comparison">[flow:nonlinear-comparison]</a> in the upper viscosity sense for $0<t<T$. There is a function $w$, smooth on $M\times(0,T)$ and continuous on $M\times[0,T)$, such that $$v\le w\le\rho,\qquad w_t=e^{-w}\Delta_{h_t}w+(1-e^{-w})R_{h_t},\qquad w(\cdot,0)=0.$$
  - Explanation: A nonlinear viscosity subsolution in the prescribed order interval has a smooth majorant solving the corresponding equality and attaining zero initial values.
- **claim-symplectic-norm-evolution** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-symplectic-evolution)): For the flow of Theorem <a href="#thm:localized-flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:localized-flow">4</a>, $$\label{disc:symplectic-evolution} \partial_tN=p(\partial\bar\partial_YN).$$ For every real smooth base function $d=d(x,t)$, with $t$ held fixed in the spatial Hessian, $$\label{disc:base-addition} p(\partial\bar\partial_Y(N+d))=\partial_tN+\Delta_{h_t}d.$$ These identities do not assume that $h_t$ is complete or has nonnegative curvature at positive time.
  - Explanation: The symplectic quadratic polynomial of the spatial Hessian equals the time derivative of the evolving dual norm. Adding a smooth base function contributes exactly its evolving Laplacian.
- **claim-disc-compactness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-disc-compactness)): For each compact $K\subset M$ and each $A_0<\infty$, there is a compact $K'\subset M$ such that every holomorphic disc $f:\overline\Delta\to M$, smooth up to the circle, satisfying $$f(\partial\Delta)\subset K,\qquad \int_\Delta f^*\omega_g\le A_0$$ has $f(\overline\Delta)\subset K'$. Every sequence of such discs has a subsequence converging holomorphically on compact subsets of $\Delta$.* *The same statements hold for discs in $Y$ with compact boundary in $Y$ and with $\int_\Delta(\pi\circ f)^*\omega_g\le A_0$. The resulting compact may depend on $K$ and $A_0$.
  - Explanation: Compact boundary control together with bounded initial-metric area confines complete disc images and gives interior holomorphic subsequential convergence. The cotangent version requires only the area of the projected disc.
- **claim-puncture-extra-integrability** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-extra-integrability)): Let $(E,k)$ be a smooth Hermitian holomorphic vector bundle on $\Delta^*=\{0<\|z\|<1\}$, with Griffiths nonnegative curvature. Suppose that $A:E\to\Delta^*\times\mathbb C^q$ is holomorphic, pointwise injective, and has bounded operator norm. If $a$ is a holomorphic section with $\int_{\Delta^*}\|a\|_k^2\,dA<\infty$, then there are $r_0>0$ and $\sigma>0$ such that $$\label{disc:extra-integrability} \int_{0<\|z\|<r_0}\|a\|_k^2\|z\|^{-\sigma}\,dA<\infty.$$ The exponent may depend on the section and the bundle data.
  - Explanation: Finite square-integrability improves to a positive weighted integrability exponent near a puncture when the bundle is Griffiths nonnegative and admits a bounded injective holomorphic map into a trivial bundle. The exponent depends on the section and bundle.
- **claim-psh-lower-anchor-compactness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-psh-compactness-bridge)): Let $\Omega_j$ be increasing open subsets exhausting a connected complex manifold $X$, and let $u_j$ be plurisubharmonic on $\Omega_j$. Assume that for each compact $K\subset X$, the functions $u_j$ are uniformly bounded above on $K$ once $K\subset\Omega_j$. Every sequence has a subsequence for which either $u_j$ tends to $-\infty$ locally uniformly from above, or $u_j$ converges in $L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}$ to a plurisubharmonic function. A lower bound $u_j(z_j)\ge-A$ at points $z_j$ in a fixed compact set excludes the first alternative along that sequence. In particular, lower bounds on moving subsets of a fixed compact set of uniformly positive measure suffice. If the $L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}$ limit is the constant $c$, then $$\limsup_j\sup_K u_j\le c$$ for every compact $K\subset X$.
  - Explanation: Locally upper-bounded plurisubharmonic sequences either collapse locally to negative infinity or have a local integrable subsequential limit. A moving lower anchor prevents collapse, and a constant limit controls compact suprema.
- **claim-disc-frames-gauss-area** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-disc-frames)): Let $f$ be a holomorphic disc in $Y$ extending past the unit circle. There is a holomorphic frame $E_f$ of $f^*TY$, smooth on the closed disc, for which $U=dG\,E_f$ satisfies $$\label{disc:frame-unitary} U^*U=I\quad\hbox{on }\partial\Delta,\qquad \sup_\Delta\\|U\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le1.$$ Let $\mathfrak g:Y\to\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}(2n,q)$ send $y$ to $dG(T_yY)$, and normalize the Plücker form by $\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}}=i\partial\bar\partial\log\det(V^*V)$ for a local holomorphic frame $V$ of its tautological plane. Then $$\label{disc:gauss-area} D(U)^2=\frac1{2\pi}\int_\Delta (\mathfrak g\circ f)^*\omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}}.$$ If $f(\partial\Delta)\subset K$ for a fixed compact $K\subset Y$, the holomorphic component matrix $J_{E_f}$ of $\mathcal J$ in this frame is bounded throughout $\Delta$ by a constant depending only on $K$ and $G$. If also $f(0)$ ranges in a compact subset $K_0$ of a fixed coordinate chart, the frame matrix at zero and its inverse are bounded by constants depending only on $K$, $K_0$, $G$ and that chart. These constants do not depend on the disc area or on an interior compact containing its image.
  - Explanation: Holomorphic disc frames can be normalized to be unitary after immersion on the boundary and contractive inside. Their boundary seminorm equals Gauss-map area, while symplectic components and center frames have bounds independent of interior area.
- **claim-boundary-spectral-factorization** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-factorization)): Let $L$ be an $r\times r$ smooth Hermitian matrix on the circle with $mI\le L\le MI$, where $0<m\le M<\infty$. There is a holomorphic invertible matrix $H$ on $\Delta$, smooth with smooth inverse on $\overline\Delta$, such that $L=H^*H$ on the circle. It satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \\|H\\|_{\infty,\mathrm{op}}&\le\sqrt M,& \\|H^{-1}\\|_{\infty,\mathrm{op}}&\le m^{-1/2},\label{disc:factor-sup}\\ D(H)^2&\le\frac{M}{2m^2}D(L)^2,& D(H^{-1})^2&\le\frac1{2m^3}D(L)^2. \label{disc:factor-seminorm} \end{aligned}$$ In particular the seminorm constants depend only on the boundary spectral bounds, independently of higher derivatives of $L$.
  - Explanation: A smooth uniformly positive Hermitian boundary matrix has a smooth holomorphic invertible factor. Both factor seminorms depend on boundary spectral bounds and the boundary matrix seminorm, independently of higher derivatives.
- **claim-disc-envelope-viscosity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-disc-inequality)): Suppose that a smooth real function $\varphi$ touches $v$ from below at $(y_*,s_*)$, where $0<s_*<T$. In holomorphic cotangent coordinates write $$W=\partial\bar\partial_Y\varphi(y_*,s_*)= \begin{pmatrix} A&B\\ B^*&C\end{pmatrix},\qquad C>0.$$ There is a Hermitian form $K$ on the horizontal coordinate space such that $$K\ge0,\qquad K\ge A-BC^{-1}B^*,\qquad \partial_s\varphi(y_*,s_*)\ge\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(C^{\mathsf T}K). \label{var:viscosity-inequality}$$ The transpose is the tangent–cotangent contraction in these coordinates. In particular the statement applies to lower tests translated in a fixed cotangent coordinate chart; its contraction has constant coefficients.
  - Explanation: A lower test of the disc envelope with positive vertical Levi block has a positive horizontal majorant of its Schur complement. Contraction of that majorant against the transposed vertical block is bounded by the test time derivative.
- **claim-disc-deformation-uniformities** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-disc-deformation)): Use the preceding localization, with $j$ sufficiently large and $\varepsilon>0$ sufficiently small that the minimizing-sequence centers have a common interior margin in $\mathcal C$. Fix a bound $L<\infty$ and such a sequence for <a href="#var:penalized-functional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="var:penalized-functional">[var:penalized-functional]</a>, with energy bounded by $E_0<\infty$. Let $l_\nu$ be any holomorphic columns, smooth on the closed disc, such that $$\\|l_\nu\\|_\infty\le L,\qquad D(l_\nu)\le D_0<\infty.$$ They may depend on the disc. With $\varphi_{E,c}=E_f(0)^*\partial\bar\partial_Y\varphi(f(0),s)E_f(0)$, one has $$\limsup_{\nu\to\infty} \left\{ h(0)^2\varphi_{E,c}(l(0),\overline{l(0)}) -\langle T_b(l,\bar l)\rangle -C_j(L)\varepsilon\bigl(1+\mathcal E+D(l)^2\bigr) \right\}\le0. \label{var:deformation-estimate}$$ The constant $C_j(L)$ depends only on $L$, the dimension, the chosen integer $a$, the fixed time interval and center cylinder, and smooth coefficient bounds for the metrics, immersion, Gauss map and cutoffs on a fixed neighborhood of the boundary compact $K_j$. It is independent of $\varepsilon,E_0,D_0$, of the interior compact $K_{j,\varepsilon}'$, and of the auxiliary loss introduced in the proof. In contrast, the existence radius of the deformations, bounds on their full coefficients, and bounds on Taylor remainders may depend on $j,L,E_0,D_0,K_{j,\varepsilon}'$, that loss, the interior margin of the centers, and the fixed smooth test $\varphi$.
  - Explanation: Disc-dependent holomorphic deformation columns obey a mixed-variation estimate whose penalty coefficient uses boundary data only. Deformation existence radii and Taylor bounds may still depend on the compact containing disc interiors.
- **claim-symplectic-schur-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-symplectic-schur)): In a cotangent vector space with canonical bivector $\mathcal J=\sum_i e_i\wedge f_i$, let $\mathcal H>0$ be Hermitian, and suppose $\mathcal H\ge W$, where the vertical block $C$ of $W$ is positive. Then $$S=\mathop{\mathrm{Schur}}(\mathcal H)\ge0,\qquad S\ge\mathop{\mathrm{Schur}}(W),\qquad p(\mathcal H)\ge\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(C^{\mathsf T}S). \label{var:schur-cost}$$
  - Explanation: A positive Hermitian majorant has a positive Schur complement dominating that of the lower form. Its symplectic cost bounds contraction of this Schur complement with the positive vertical block of the lower form.
- **claim-ellipsoid-optimizer-contacts** (construction, [source](#stmt-ell-optimizer-contacts)): The maximum in <a href="#ell:optimizer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ell:optimizer">[ell:optimizer]</a> exists and is unique. The function $\mathcal L$ is lower semicontinuous in each holomorphic coordinate chart, and $$\label{ell:volume-gap} F_0:=\log\det b_t-\mathcal L \quad\text{satisfies}\quad 0\le F_0\le\rho.$$ At any fixed point, choose linear cotangent coordinates in which $B=I$. There are finitely many unit columns $\xi_i$ and positive numbers $\alpha_i$ such that $$\label{ell:contact-resolution} v(x,\xi_i,t)=1,\qquad \sum_{i=1}^m\alpha_i\xi_i\xi_i^*=I, \qquad \sum_{i=1}^m\alpha_i=n.$$ The largest weight can be made arbitrarily small by repeating columns.
  - Explanation: The determinant-maximizing feasible fiber matrix exists uniquely and has a lower semicontinuous logarithmic determinant. Normalized contact vectors resolve the identity with positive weights, and repetition reduces the largest weight without changing the resolution.
- **claim-ellipsoid-volume-viscosity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-ellipsoid-comparison)): For the optimizer in <a href="#ell:optimizer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ell:optimizer">[ell:optimizer]</a>, every smooth lower test $\lambda$ of $\mathcal L$ at an interior point $(x_0,t_0)$ satisfies $$\label{ell:volume-viscosity} \partial_t\lambda(x_0,t_0) \ge e^{-F_0(x_0,t_0)}\Delta_{h_{t_0}}\lambda(x_0,t_0).$$ Thus $\mathcal L$ satisfies this inequality in the lower viscosity sense on $M\times(0,T)$.
  - Explanation: The logarithmic determinant of the fiber optimizer satisfies a lower viscosity parabolic inequality. Simultaneous contact jets retain a common base and positive vertical blocks before their Schur bounds are aggregated.
- **claim-joint-cotangent-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-joint-positivity)): For the flow in Theorem <a href="#thm:localized-flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:localized-flow">4</a>, the function $$(x,\xi,w)\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27FC}% {\let\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F6}% \longrightarrow% \EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}% }\longrightarrow\longmapsto}% \EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}% }N(x,\xi,e^{w+\bar w})$$ is plurisubharmonic on $T^{*(1,0)}M\times\mathbb C$. For every finite $T$, its disc envelope satisfies $v=N$ on $T^{*(1,0)}M\times(0,T)$.
  - Explanation: The evolving dual norm is jointly plurisubharmonic in the cotangent variables and complexified logarithmic time. Its disc envelope equals the norm on every finite positive-time horizon.
- **claim-harnack-and-completeness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-harnack-completeness)): The flow of Theorem <a href="#thm:localized-flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:localized-flow">4</a> has nonnegative holomorphic bisectional curvature and is complete at every positive time. Its Ricci tensor is positive definite. Writing $A=\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(h_t)$ and $R=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{h_t}A$, one has, in fixed holomorphic coordinates, $$\begin{aligned} A_t+A h_t^{-1}A+t^{-1}A&\ge0, \label{chart:matrix-harnack}\\ R_t+t^{-1}R+2\operatorname{Re}\partial_XR+A(X,\overline X)&\ge0 \qquad(X\in T^{1,0}M). \label{chart:scalar-harnack} \end{aligned}$$ In particular $h_{t'}\le h_t$ for $t'\ge t$, $R(o,t)\le C$ for $t\ge0$, and $$\label{chart:integrated-harnack} R(x,t)\le R(y,t')\frac{t'}t \exp\!\left(\frac{C\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_{h_t}(x,y)^2}{t'-t}\right) \qquad(0<t<t').$$ On each fixed compact set, $h_t\le C_Kt^{-a_K}g$ for $t\ge1$, with $a_K>0$.
  - Explanation: Joint positivity yields nonnegative bisectional curvature, positive Ricci curvature, matrix and scalar Harnack inequalities, and contraction on fixed compact sets. The integrated inequality is established before it is used to prove positive-time completeness.
- **claim-uniform-immersed-charts** (construction, [source](#stmt-chart-local-charts)): For every fixed compact $K\subset M$, there are $r_K,c_K,C_K>0$ such that, for sufficiently large $t$ and every $p\in K$, there is a holomorphic local biholomorphism $$\Phi:B_{\mathbb C^n}(r_K)\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F6}% \longrightarrow% \EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}% }M,\qquad \Phi(0)=p,\qquad d\Phi(0)\text{ unitary for }h_t,$$ with $c_Kg_{\rm eucl}\le\Phi^*h_t\le C_Kg_{\rm eucl}$. The pullback metrics have bounds of every fixed derivative order on smaller balls. The corresponding local smooth compactness statement holds for rescaled flows whenever curvature is uniformly bounded on fixed-radius moving balls during a fixed two-sided time interval.
  - Explanation: Late-time compact subsets admit uniform immersed holomorphic charts with unitary center derivative, metric equivalence, and higher derivative bounds. The construction requires no injectivity-radius hypothesis.
- **claim-static-chart-distortion** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-static-distortion)): Fix $r,c>0$ and a compact set $K\subset M$. Suppose $\Phi:B_{\mathbb C^n}(r)\to M$ is a holomorphic local biholomorphism, $\Phi(0)\in K$, and $\widetilde g=\Phi^*g\ge c g_{\rm eucl}$. Let $A$ and $B$ be the least and greatest singular lengths of $d\Phi(0)$, using the Euclidean metric in the domain and $g$ in the target. There are constants depending only on $K,r,c$ and the initial geometry on a compact neighborhood of $K$ such that $$\label{chart:static-distortion} B\le C(1+A)^C.$$ In particular these constants are independent of the particular chart and its total distortion.
  - Explanation: For immersed charts with a fixed pullback lower metric bound and centers in a compact set, the largest initial-metric singular length is bounded by a fixed power of the smallest. The constants do not depend on the chart's total distortion.
- **claim-volume-clock-ricci-windows** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-ricci-windows)): The function $q$ is nondecreasing, $q\ge c>0$ for large $s$, and $$\label{chart:q-growth} s\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F6}% \longrightarrow% \EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}% }\infty,\qquad q\le Ce^\tau,\qquad \liminf_{s\to\infty}\frac{q(s)}s\le2.$$ Uniformly on every fixed compact set, $\rho(x,t)/s\to1$, and there is $c_K>0$ such that, eventually on that compact set, $$\label{chart:metric-decay} e^{-2s}g\le h_t\le e^{-c_Ks}g.$$ There are fixed $a_0\in(0,1)$ and $b_0>0$ such that the least eigenvalue at $o$ of $t\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(h_t)$ relative to $h_t$ satisfies $$\label{chart:ricci-lower} \lambda_{\min}(s)\ge b_0q(a_0s) \quad\text{for all sufficiently large }s.$$ Moreover there are $S_i\to\infty$ and $K_0<\infty$ with $$\label{chart:good-windows} q(2S_i)\le K_0q(a_0S_i).$$ One can choose $s_i\in[5S_i/4,7S_i/4]$ so that $(\log q)'(s_i)\to0$. If $t_i$ corresponds to $s_i$ and $R_i=R(o,t_i)$, the rescaled flows $$\label{chart:rescaled-flows} g_i(z)=R_i h_{t_i+z/R_i}$$ have uniform curvature and derivative bounds on each fixed-radius moving ball about $o$ in a fixed small two-sided time interval. At $(o,0)$ their scalar curvature is one and their Ricci eigenvalues have a fixed positive lower bound.
  - Explanation: The volume clock has monotone positive speed and controlled lower asymptotic growth. Compact metric decay and a delayed lower Ricci bound permit selection of windows with uniformly controlled rescaled geometry and positive Ricci pinching at the center.
- **claim-simple-connectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-simple-connectivity)): The manifold $M$ is simply connected.
  - Explanation: Selected Harnack limits force strict real concavity of scalar curvature near the center. On actual intrinsic balls, the resulting gradient flow shortens carried loops until they contract in a normal ball.
- **claim-psh-liouville** (theorem, [source](#stmt-chart-psh-liouville)): Every bounded-above plurisubharmonic function on $M$ is constant, including a function which is not assumed continuous.
  - Explanation: Bounded-above plurisubharmonic functions are constant without a continuity assumption. The proof uses convexity of compact-ball maxima in logarithmic radius and strict radial complex Hessian comparison.
- **claim-coherent-shrinking-charts** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-shrinking-charts)): For every sufficiently large volume clock $s$, there is a holomorphic local biholomorphism $\Phi_s:B_{\mathbb C^n}(r_0)\to M$ centered at $o$, with unitary center derivative for the metric at clock $s$, and $$\label{chart:uniform-chart-metric} C^{-1}g_{\rm eucl}\le\Phi_s^*h_{t(s)}\le Cg_{\rm eucl}.$$ For fixed $0<r_1<r_0$, chosen sufficiently small, the transition germs $F_{s,u}=\Phi_u^{-1}\Phi_s$, $u\ge s$, extend to nonsingular holomorphic maps on $B(r_1)$ with a common bound. They fix $0$, compose as germs, and satisfy $$\label{chart:center-transitions} \\|F_{s,u}'(0)\\|\le1,\qquad \|\det F_{s,u}'(0)\|=e^{-(u-s)/2},\qquad \\|F_{s,u}'(0)^{-1}\\|\le e^{(u-s)/2}.$$ There are coherent inverse branches $f_s$ on neighborhoods of any fixed compact exhaustion set for all sufficiently large $s$, satisfying $f_s(o)=0$ and $\Phi_sf_s=\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}$. A constant $\sigma>0$, common to all compact sets, satisfies $$\label{chart:common-exponent} \sup_K\|f_s\|\le e^{-\sigma s} \quad\text{for every fixed compact }K \text{ and all sufficiently large }s.$$ For every fixed jet order $d$ there is $C_d$ such that $$\label{chart:transition-jets} \|J_dF_{s,u}\|\le C_d\min\{1,e^{C_ds-\sigma u}\} \qquad(u\ge s\text{ sufficiently large}).$$ There are $c,\gamma_0>0$ such that, for each fixed $0<\gamma\le\gamma_0$, one has $$\label{chart:transition-small-balls} \sup_{\|z\|\le e^{-\gamma s}}\|F_{s,u}(z)\| \le e^{-c\gamma u} \qquad\text{for every }u\ge s\ge s_\gamma.$$ The constants in the last two estimates are independent of the later endpoint $u$; the starting threshold in <a href="#chart:common-exponent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="chart:common-exponent">[chart:common-exponent]</a> may depend on $K$.
  - Explanation: The immersed charts have bounded nonsingular transition maps and coherent inverse branches on exhausting domains. A single contraction exponent works on every fixed compact set, with jet and shrinking-ball bounds uniform in the later endpoint.
- **claim-polynomial-transition-models** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-polynomial-models)): Fix an integer $d\ge2$ and a macro width $0<h<1$. After starting at a sufficiently late macro endpoint, there is a grid $s_k\to\infty$ with macro endpoints $S_j=e^{hj}$ and the following properties. Each macro interval is either one bad step or is divided into regular steps of length comparable to $\sqrt{S_j}$. Thus, eventually, $$c_h\sqrt{s_k}\le\Delta s_k:=s_{k+1}-s_k \le(e^h-1)s_k. \label{eq:dyn:grid-size}$$ For $F_k=F_{s_k,s_{k+1}}$ there are polynomial local coordinate changes $H_k$ and polynomial automorphisms $G_k$ of $\mathbb C^n$ satisfying $$J_d(H_{k+1}\circ F_k)=J_d(G_k\circ H_k). \label{eq:dyn:jet-conjugacy}$$ The degrees of $H_k,G_k,G_k^{-1}$ are bounded independently of $k$. The coefficients of $H_k$ and its inverse jet are $\exp(o(s_k))$; $H_k'(0)$ and its inverse have polynomial bounds in $s_k$. For a constant $C_d$ depending only on the fixed jet order and dimension, $$\begin{aligned} \lVert G_k\rVert_{\mathrm{coeff}}+\lVert G_k^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{coeff}} &\le \exp\bigl(C_d\Delta s_k+o(s_k)\bigr), \label{eq:dyn:coefficient-bound}\\ \lVert G_k'(0)^{-1}\rVert &\le s_k^{C}\exp(\Delta s_k/2). \label{eq:dyn:center-inverse} \end{aligned}$$ In particular, the coefficient $C_d$ of $\Delta s_k$ is independent of $h$. Finally, for some finite $D$, $$\deg G_{0,k}\le D s_k \quad\text{on an unbounded subsequence of macro endpoints.} \label{eq:dyn:forward-degree}$$
  - Explanation: A late clock grid supports polynomial coordinate corrections and polynomial automorphisms matching transition jets. Their degrees are uniformly bounded, their coefficients have controlled growth, and forward composition degrees admit a linear bound along an unbounded subsequence.
- **claim-constant-volume-jet-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-volume-jets)): For fixed $n,d$, every invertible constant-volume $d$-jet fixing zero is the $d$-jet of a polynomial automorphism of $\mathbb C^n$. Its degree and inverse degree are bounded by a constant $D_d$. Its coefficients and inverse coefficients are bounded polynomially in the coefficients of the given jet and its inverse linear part. These bounds do not depend on the grid or on $h$.
  - Explanation: Finite constant-volume jets are realized by polynomial automorphisms using divergence-free shear corrections. Degree bounds depend on dimension and jet order; coefficient bounds also depend polynomially on the input jet and its inverse linear part.
- **claim-weight-compatible-linear-alignment** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-linear-alignment)): On every run of regular steps there are unipotent matrices $L_k^+$, with off-diagonal entries only in rows of smaller weight than their columns, such that, on putting $L_k^-=D_k^{-1}L_k^+D_k$, the diagonal step derivative remains $D_k$ and every regular jump becomes $$J_k=L_{k+1}^-U_k(L_k^+)^{-1},\qquad (J_k)_{il}=0\quad\hbox{if }w_{k+1,i}<w_{k,l}. \label{eq:dyn:aligned-jump}$$ The matrices $L_k^+$ and their inverses have polynomial bounds in the macro index $j$, while $$\lVert L_k^- -I\rVert \le j^C\exp\left(-c\frac{\sqrt{S_j}}{j^2}\right). \label{eq:dyn:small-start-change}$$ The estimates are uniform in finite terminal horizons, and hold on an infinite regular run by a coefficientwise limit.
  - Explanation: Unipotent endpoint changes remove weight-decreasing entries from regular linear jumps while retaining the diagonal step derivative. Backward finite-horizon constructions have uniform bounds and admit coefficientwise limits on infinite runs.
- **claim-forward-degree-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-degree-control)): The flag threshold $M$ can be chosen so that regular macro starts occur arbitrarily late, and the polynomial models constructed above, initialized at any sufficiently late such start, satisfy <a href="#eq:dyn:forward-degree" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dyn:forward-degree">[eq:dyn:forward-degree]</a>.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently large flag threshold makes completed bad components pay for their degree inflation through clock-speed growth. Bad prefixes have a controlled remaining cost, and regular starts occur arbitrarily late.
- **claim-corrected-coordinate-backward-limit** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-unif-backward-limit)): For either raw sequence above and every sufficiently large $k$, $$\xi_k=\lim_{l\to\infty}G_{k,l}^{-1}\zeta_l$$ exists locally uniformly in the indicated variables. It is holomorphic and satisfies $$\lvert\xi_k-\zeta_k\rvert\le e^{-6s_k},\qquad \xi_{k+1}=G_k\xi_k. \label{eq:unif:shadow}$$ All applications of <a href="#eq:unif:inverse-tube" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:unif:inverse-tube">[eq:unif:inverse-tube]</a> take place inside its stated balls.
  - Explanation: Backward polynomial iteration converges locally uniformly for both prescribed raw-coordinate regimes. A summable defect estimate closes an induction that keeps every inverse evaluation inside its valid tube.
- **claim-global-open-image-coordinates** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-global-coordinates)): The locally uniform limit on the exhaustion $$\Psi(x)=\lim_{l\to\infty}G_{0,l}^{-1}H_lf_{s_l}(x) \label{eq:unif:global-map}$$ defines an injective holomorphic map $M\to\mathbb C^n$ with $\Psi(o)=0$. Its image $\Omega$ is open, and $\Psi:M\to\Omega$ is a biholomorphism. Moreover, on every compact $K\subset\Omega$, $$\sup_K\lvert G_{0,k}\rvert\le e^{-(\sigma/2)s_k} \quad\hbox{eventually}, \label{eq:unif:forward-smallness}$$ and, at every sufficiently large $k$, $$G_{0,k}^{-1}\bigl(B(e^{-\gamma's_k})\bigr)\subset\Omega. \label{eq:unif:inverse-ball-inclusion}$$
  - Explanation: The corrected inverse charts define a globally injective nonsingular holomorphic map onto an open image. Forward smallness on compact image sets and inverse-ball inclusion supply the additional controls used to prove that this image is all of complex Euclidean space.

        "exact_quote": "There is a smooth Kähler–Ricci flow $h_t$ on $M$, for $0\\le t<\\infty$, with $h_0=g$.         "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large fixed integer $k$, there is a positive function $\\chi(x,t)$ on $M\\times[0,T]$, smooth in space and continuously differentiable in time one-sided at the initial surface, such that $$\\label{flow:cutoff-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n C_T^{-1}u^{-k}\\le\\chi\\le C_Tu^{-k},\\qquad\n |\\partial\\chi|_g\\le C_T\\chi/u,\\\\\n \\partial\\bar\\partial\\chi\\le C_T\\chi h_t,\n \\qquad \\partial_t\\chi\\le C_T(1+t^{\\beta-1})\\chi\n \\quad(t>0).\n \\end{gathered}$$",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-flow-cutoff-lemma",
          "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large fixed integer $k$, there is a positive function $\\chi(x,t)$ on $M\\times[0,T]$, smooth in space and continuously differentiable in time one-sided at the initial surface, such that $$\\label{flow:cutoff-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n C_T^{-1}u^{-k}\\le\\chi\\le C_Tu^{-k},\\qquad\n |\\partial\\chi|_g\\le C_T\\chi/u,\\\\\n \\partial\\bar\\partial\\chi\\le C_T\\chi h_t,\n \\qquad \\partial_t\\chi\\le C_T(1+t^{\\beta-1})\\chi\n \\quad(t>0).\n \\end{gathered}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-scalar-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite $T>0$. The following conclusions hold for the flow of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:localized-flow\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:localized-flow\">4</a>.*\n\n\n\n1.  *The function $\\rho$ is the minimal nonnegative solution with zero initial values of $$\\label{flow:rho-source}\n          (\\partial_t-\\Delta_{h_t})\\rho\n                            =\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{h_t}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}(g).$$ There are nonnegative smooth supercaloric functions $\\rho_j$ on $M\\times[0,T]$ such that $$\\label{flow:rho-tails}\n          0\\le\\rho_j\\le\\rho,\\qquad\n          \\rho_j\\ge\\rho-C_j,\\qquad\n          \\rho_j\\mathrel{%\n      \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F6}%\n      \\longrightarrow%\n      \\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}%\n    }0\n          \\text{ locally uniformly on }M\\times[0,T].$$ Each fixed $\\rho_j$ has all spatial and one-sided time derivatives smooth at $t=0$ and bounded on compact spacetime sets.*\n\n\n2.  *Suppose $v$ is upper semicontinuous, bounded on compact spacetime sets, $0\\le v\\le\\rho$, and satisfies, in the upper viscosity sense on $0<t<T$, $$\\label{flow:nonlinear-comparison}\n          \\partial_tv\\le e^{-v}\\Delta_{h_t}v+(1-e^{-v})R_{h_t}.$$ Then $v=0$ on $M\\times[0,T)$.*\n\n*Neither assertion requires completeness of $h_t$.",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "exact_quote": "Fix $T>0$ and let $v$ be upper semicontinuous, bounded on compact spacetime sets, and satisfy $0\\le v\\le\\rho$ on $M\\times[0,T]$.         "exact_quote": "For the flow of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:localized-flow\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:localized-flow\">4</a>, $$\\label{disc:symplectic-evolution}\n \\partial_tN=p(\\partial\\bar\\partial_YN).$$ For every real smooth base function $d=d(x,t)$, with $t$ held fixed in the spatial Hessian, $$\\label{disc:base-addition}\n p(\\partial\\bar\\partial_Y(N+d))=\\partial_tN+\\Delta_{h_t}d.$$ These identities do not assume that $h_t$ is complete or has nonnegative curvature at positive time.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-disc-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "For each compact $K\\subset M$ and each $A_0<\\infty$, there is a compact $K'\\subset M$ such that every holomorphic disc $f:\\overline\\Delta\\to M$, smooth up to the circle, satisfying $$f(\\partial\\Delta)\\subset K,\\qquad\n \\int_\\Delta f^*\\omega_g\\le A_0$$ has $f(\\overline\\Delta)\\subset K'$. Every sequence of such discs has a subsequence converging holomorphically on compact subsets of $\\Delta$.*\n\n*The same statements hold for discs in $Y$ with compact boundary in $Y$ and with $\\int_\\Delta(\\pi\\circ f)^*\\omega_g\\le A_0$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-extra-integrability",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(E,k)$ be a smooth Hermitian holomorphic vector bundle on $\\Delta^*=\\{0<|z|<1\\}$, with Griffiths nonnegative curvature.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-psh-compactness-bridge",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega_j$ be increasing open subsets exhausting a connected complex manifold $X$, and let $u_j$ be plurisubharmonic on $\\Omega_j$. Assume that for each compact $K\\subset X$, the functions $u_j$ are uniformly bounded above on $K$ once $K\\subset\\Omega_j$. Every sequence has a subsequence for which either $u_j$ tends to $-\\infty$ locally uniformly from above, or $u_j$ converges in $L^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ to a plurisubharmonic function. A lower bound $u_j(z_j)\\ge-A$ at points $z_j$ in a fixed compact set excludes the first alternative along that sequence. In particular, lower bounds on moving subsets of a fixed compact set of uniformly positive measure suffice. If the $L^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ limit is the constant $c$, then $$\\limsup_j\\sup_K u_j\\le c$$ for every compact $K\\subset X$.",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },

        {

A lower bound $u_j(z_j)\\ge-A$ at points $z_j$ in a fixed compact set excludes the first alternative along that sequence.           "source_id": "PM-338.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-disc-frames",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f$ be a holomorphic disc in $Y$ extending past the unit circle. There is a holomorphic frame $E_f$ of $f^*TY$, smooth on the closed disc, for which $U=dG\\,E_f$ satisfies $$\\label{disc:frame-unitary}\n U^*U=I\\quad\\hbox{on }\\partial\\Delta,\\qquad\n \\sup_\\Delta\\|U\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\le1.$$ Let $\\mathfrak g:Y\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}(2n,q)$ send $y$ to $dG(T_yY)$, and normalize the Plücker form by $\\omega_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}}=i\\partial\\bar\\partial\\log\\det(V^*V)$ for a local holomorphic frame $V$ of its tautological plane. Then $$\\label{disc:gauss-area}\n D(U)^2=\\frac1{2\\pi}\\int_\\Delta\n                          (\\mathfrak g\\circ f)^*\\omega_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}}.$$ If $f(\\partial\\Delta)\\subset K$ for a fixed compact $K\\subset Y$, the holomorphic component matrix $J_{E_f}$ of $\\mathcal J$ in this frame is bounded throughout $\\Delta$ by a constant depending only on $K$ and $G$. If also $f(0)$ ranges in a compact subset $K_0$ of a fixed coordinate chart, the frame matrix at zero and its inverse are bounded by constants depending only on $K$, $K_0$, $G$ and that chart. These constants do not depend on the disc area or on an interior compact containing its image.",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-factorization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be an $r\\times r$ smooth Hermitian matrix on the circle with $mI\\le L\\le MI$, where $0<m\\le M<\\infty$. There is a holomorphic invertible matrix $H$ on $\\Delta$, smooth with smooth inverse on $\\overline\\Delta$, such that $L=H^*H$ on the circle. It satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|H\\|_{\\infty,\\mathrm{op}}&\\le\\sqrt M,&\n \\|H^{-1}\\|_{\\infty,\\mathrm{op}}&\\le m^{-1/2},\\label{disc:factor-sup}\\\\\n D(H)^2&\\le\\frac{M}{2m^2}D(L)^2,&\n D(H^{-1})^2&\\le\\frac1{2m^3}D(L)^2.\n \\label{disc:factor-seminorm}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular the seminorm constants depend only on the boundary spectral bounds, independently of higher derivatives of $L$.",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-factorization",

          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-disc-inequality",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that a smooth real function $\\varphi$ touches $v$ from below at $(y_*,s_*)$, where $0<s_*<T$. In holomorphic cotangent coordinates write $$W=\\partial\\bar\\partial_Y\\varphi(y_*,s_*)=\n \\begin{pmatrix} A&B\\\\ B^*&C\\end{pmatrix},\\qquad C>0.$$ There is a Hermitian form $K$ on the horizontal coordinate space such that $$K\\ge0,\\qquad K\\ge A-BC^{-1}B^*,\\qquad\n \\partial_s\\varphi(y_*,s_*)\\ge\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(C^{\\mathsf T}K).\n \\label{var:viscosity-inequality}$$ The transpose is the tangent–cotangent contraction in these coordinates.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-disc-deformation",
        "exact_quote": "Use the preceding localization, with $j$ sufficiently large and $\\varepsilon>0$ sufficiently small that the minimizing-sequence centers have a common interior margin in $\\mathcal C$. Fix a bound $L<\\infty$ and such a sequence for <a href=\"#var:penalized-functional\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"var:penalized-functional\">[var:penalized-functional]</a>, with energy bounded by $E_0<\\infty$. Let $l_\\nu$ be any holomorphic columns, smooth on the closed disc, such that $$\\|l_\\nu\\|_\\infty\\le L,\\qquad D(l_\\nu)\\le D_0<\\infty.$$ They may depend on the disc. With $\\varphi_{E,c}=E_f(0)^*\\partial\\bar\\partial_Y\\varphi(f(0),s)E_f(0)$, one has $$\\limsup_{\\nu\\to\\infty}\n \\left\\{\n h(0)^2\\varphi_{E,c}(l(0),\\overline{l(0)})\n -\\langle T_b(l,\\bar l)\\rangle\n -C_j(L)\\varepsilon\\bigl(1+\\mathcal E+D(l)^2\\bigr)\n \\right\\}\\le0.\n \\label{var:deformation-estimate}$$ The constant $C_j(L)$ depends only on $L$, the dimension, the chosen integer $a$, the fixed time interval and center cylinder, and smooth coefficient bounds for the metrics, immersion, Gauss map and cutoffs on a fixed neighborhood of the boundary compact $K_j$. It is independent of $\\varepsilon,E_0,D_0$, of the interior compact $K_{j,\\varepsilon}'$, and of the auxiliary loss introduced in the proof. In contrast, the existence radius of the deformations, bounds on their full coefficients, and bounds on Taylor remainders may depend on $j,L,E_0,D_0,K_{j,\\varepsilon}'$, that loss, the interior margin of the centers, and the fixed smooth test $\\varphi$.",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-disc-deformation",
          "exact_quote": "Use the preceding localization, with $j$ sufficiently large and $\\varepsilon>0$ sufficiently small that the minimizing-sequence centers have a common interior margin in $\\mathcal C$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-symplectic-schur",
        "exact_quote": "In a cotangent vector space with canonical bivector $\\mathcal J=\\sum_i e_i\\wedge f_i$, let $\\mathcal H>0$ be Hermitian, and suppose $\\mathcal H\\ge W$, where the vertical block $C$ of $W$ is positive. Then $$S=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Schur}}(\\mathcal H)\\ge0,\\qquad\n S\\ge\\mathop{\\mathrm{Schur}}(W),\\qquad p(\\mathcal H)\\ge\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(C^{\\mathsf T}S).\n \\label{var:schur-cost}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-symplectic-schur",
          "exact_quote": "In a cotangent vector space with canonical bivector $\\mathcal J=\\sum_i e_i\\wedge f_i$, let $\\mathcal H>0$ be Hermitian, and suppose $\\mathcal H\\ge W$, where the vertical block $C$ of $W$ is positive.           "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "In a cotangent vector space with canonical bivector $\\mathcal J=\\sum_i e_i\\wedge f_i$, let $\\mathcal H>0$ be Hermitian, and suppose $\\mathcal H\\ge W$, where the vertical block $C$ of $W$ is positive.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ell-optimizer-contacts",
        "exact_quote": "The maximum in <a href=\"#ell:optimizer\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ell:optimizer\">[ell:optimizer]</a> exists and is unique. The function $\\mathcal L$ is lower semicontinuous in each holomorphic coordinate chart, and $$\\label{ell:volume-gap}\n F_0:=\\log\\det b_t-\\mathcal L\n \\quad\\text{satisfies}\\quad 0\\le F_0\\le\\rho.$$ At any fixed point, choose linear cotangent coordinates in which $B=I$. There are finitely many unit columns $\\xi_i$ and positive numbers $\\alpha_i$ such that $$\\label{ell:contact-resolution}\n v(x,\\xi_i,t)=1,\\qquad\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\alpha_i\\xi_i\\xi_i^*=I,\n \\qquad \\sum_{i=1}^m\\alpha_i=n.$$ The largest weight can be made arbitrarily small by repeating columns.",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ell-optimizer-contacts",
          "exact_quote": "The maximum in <a href=\"#ell:optimizer\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ell:optimizer\">[ell:optimizer]</a> exists and is unique. The function $\\mathcal L$ is lower semicontinuous in each holomorphic coordinate chart, and $$\\label{ell:volume-gap}\n F_0:=\\log\\det b_t-\\mathcal L\n \\quad\\text{satisfies}\\quad 0\\le F_0\\le\\rho.$$ At any fixed point, choose linear cotangent coordinates in which $B=I$. There are finitely many unit columns $\\xi_i$ and positive numbers $\\alpha_i$ such that $$\\label{ell:contact-resolution}\n v(x,\\xi_i,t)=1,\\qquad\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\alpha_i\\xi_i\\xi_i^*=I,\n \\qquad \\sum_{i=1}^m\\alpha_i=n.$$ The largest weight can be made arbitrarily small by repeating columns.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The maximum in <a href=\"#ell:optimizer\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ell:optimizer\">[ell:optimizer]</a> exists and is unique. The function $\\mathcal L$ is lower semicontinuous in each holomorphic coordinate chart, and $$\\label{ell:volume-gap}\n F_0:=\\log\\det b_t-\\mathcal L\n \\quad\\text{satisfies}\\quad 0\\le F_0\\le\\rho.$$ At any fixed point, choose linear cotangent coordinates in which $B=I$. There are finitely many unit columns $\\xi_i$ and positive numbers $\\alpha_i$ such that $$\\label{ell:contact-resolution}\n v(x,\\xi_i,t)=1,\\qquad\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\alpha_i\\xi_i\\xi_i^*=I,\n \\qquad \\sum_{i=1}^m\\alpha_i=n.$$ The largest weight can be made arbitrarily small by repeating columns.",
      "explanation": "The determinant-maximizing feasible fiber matrix exists uniquely and has a lower semicontinuous logarithmic determinant. Normalized contact vectors resolve the identity with positive weights, and repetition reduces the largest weight without changing the resolution.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "ell:optimizer",
          "exact_quote": "For each $(x,t)$, let $B(x,t)$ be the feasible matrix maximizing the determinant: $$\\label{ell:optimizer}\n \\det B(x,t)=\\max\\bigl\\{\\det D:\n D>0,\\ \\xi^*D\\xi\\le v(x,\\xi,t)\\text{ for every }\\xi\\bigr\\},\n \\qquad \\mathcal L=\\log\\det B.$$ The ellipsoid $\\{\\xi:\\xi^*D\\xi<1\\}$ of a feasible matrix contains $\\{\\xi:v(x,\\xi,t)<1\\}$. Maximizing $\\det D$ minimizes its Euclidean volume in these fiber coordinates.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-ellipsoid-optimizer-contacts",
      "kind": "construction",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ell-optimizer-contacts",
          "exact_quote": "At any fixed point,",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-ellipsoid-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "For the optimizer in <a href=\"#ell:optimizer\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ell:optimizer\">[ell:optimizer]</a>, every smooth lower test $\\lambda$ of $\\mathcal L$ at an interior point $(x_0,t_0)$ satisfies $$\\label{ell:volume-viscosity}\n \\partial_t\\lambda(x_0,t_0)\n \\ge e^{-F_0(x_0,t_0)}\\Delta_{h_{t_0}}\\lambda(x_0,t_0).$$ Thus $\\mathcal L$ satisfies this inequality in the lower viscosity sense on $M\\times(0,T)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-harnack-completeness",
        "exact_quote": "The flow of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:localized-flow\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:localized-flow\">4</a> has nonnegative holomorphic bisectional curvature and is complete at every positive time. Its Ricci tensor is positive definite. Writing $A=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}(h_t)$ and $R=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{h_t}A$, one has, in fixed holomorphic coordinates, $$\\begin{aligned}\n A_t+A h_t^{-1}A+t^{-1}A&\\ge0,\n \\label{chart:matrix-harnack}\\\\\n R_t+t^{-1}R+2\\operatorname{Re}\\partial_XR+A(X,\\overline X)&\\ge0\n \\qquad(X\\in T^{1,0}M).\n \\label{chart:scalar-harnack}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular $h_{t'}\\le h_t$ for $t'\\ge t$, $R(o,t)\\le C$ for $t\\ge0$, and $$\\label{chart:integrated-harnack}\n R(x,t)\\le R(y,t')\\frac{t'}t\n \\exp\\!\\left(\\frac{C\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_{h_t}(x,y)^2}{t'-t}\\right)\n \\qquad(0<t<t').$$ On each fixed compact set, $h_t\\le C_Kt^{-a_K}g$ for $t\\ge1$, with $a_K>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-harnack-completeness",
          "exact_quote": "The flow of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:localized-flow\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:localized-flow\">4</a> has nonnegative holomorphic bisectional curvature and is complete at every positive time.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-chart-local-charts",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed compact $K\\subset M$, there are $r_K,c_K,C_K>0$ such that, for sufficiently large $t$ and every $p\\in K$, there is a holomorphic local biholomorphism $$\\Phi:B_{\\mathbb C^n}(r_K)\\mathrel{%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F6}%\n  \\longrightarrow%\n  \\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}%\n}M,\\qquad\n \\Phi(0)=p,\\qquad d\\Phi(0)\\text{ unitary for }h_t,$$ with $c_Kg_{\\rm eucl}\\le\\Phi^*h_t\\le C_Kg_{\\rm eucl}$. The pullback metrics have bounds of every fixed derivative order on smaller balls. The corresponding local smooth compactness statement holds for rescaled flows whenever curvature is uniformly bounded on fixed-radius moving balls during a fixed two-sided time interval.",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-static-distortion",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $r,c>0$ and a compact set $K\\subset M$. Suppose $\\Phi:B_{\\mathbb C^n}(r)\\to M$ is a holomorphic local biholomorphism, $\\Phi(0)\\in K$, and $\\widetilde g=\\Phi^*g\\ge c g_{\\rm eucl}$. Let $A$ and $B$ be the least and greatest singular lengths of $d\\Phi(0)$, using the Euclidean metric in the domain and $g$ in the target. There are constants depending only on $K,r,c$ and the initial geometry on a compact neighborhood of $K$ such that $$\\label{chart:static-distortion}\n B\\le C(1+A)^C.$$ In particular these constants are independent of the particular chart and its total distortion.",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-static-distortion",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $r,c>0$ and a compact set $K\\subset M$.         }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-ricci-windows",
        "exact_quote": "The function $q$ is nondecreasing, $q\\ge c>0$ for large $s$, and $$\\label{chart:q-growth}\n s\\mathrel{%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F6}%\n  \\longrightarrow%\n  \\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}%\n}\\infty,\\qquad\n q\\le Ce^\\tau,\\qquad\n \\liminf_{s\\to\\infty}\\frac{q(s)}s\\le2.$$ Uniformly on every fixed compact set, $\\rho(x,t)/s\\to1$, and there is $c_K>0$ such that, eventually on that compact set, $$\\label{chart:metric-decay}\n e^{-2s}g\\le h_t\\le e^{-c_Ks}g.$$ There are fixed $a_0\\in(0,1)$ and $b_0>0$ such that the least eigenvalue at $o$ of $t\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}(h_t)$ relative to $h_t$ satisfies $$\\label{chart:ricci-lower}\n \\lambda_{\\min}(s)\\ge b_0q(a_0s)\n \\quad\\text{for all sufficiently large }s.$$ Moreover there are $S_i\\to\\infty$ and $K_0<\\infty$ with $$\\label{chart:good-windows}\n q(2S_i)\\le K_0q(a_0S_i).$$ One can choose $s_i\\in[5S_i/4,7S_i/4]$ so that $(\\log q)'(s_i)\\to0$. If $t_i$ corresponds to $s_i$ and $R_i=R(o,t_i)$, the rescaled flows $$\\label{chart:rescaled-flows}\n g_i(z)=R_i h_{t_i+z/R_i}$$ have uniform curvature and derivative bounds on each fixed-radius moving ball about $o$ in a fixed small two-sided time interval. At $(o,0)$ their scalar curvature is one and their Ricci eigenvalues have a fixed positive lower bound.",

      "exact_statement": "The function $q$ is nondecreasing, $q\\ge c>0$ for large $s$, and $$\\label{chart:q-growth}\n s\\mathrel{%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F6}%\n  \\longrightarrow%\n  \\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}%\n}\\infty,\\qquad\n q\\le Ce^\\tau,\\qquad\n \\liminf_{s\\to\\infty}\\frac{q(s)}s\\le2.$$ Uniformly on every fixed compact set, $\\rho(x,t)/s\\to1$, and there is $c_K>0$ such that, eventually on that compact set, $$\\label{chart:metric-decay}\n e^{-2s}g\\le h_t\\le e^{-c_Ks}g.$$ There are fixed $a_0\\in(0,1)$ and $b_0>0$ such that the least eigenvalue at $o$ of $t\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}(h_t)$ relative to $h_t$ satisfies $$\\label{chart:ricci-lower}\n \\lambda_{\\min}(s)\\ge b_0q(a_0s)\n \\quad\\text{for all sufficiently large }s.$$ Moreover there are $S_i\\to\\infty$ and $K_0<\\infty$ with $$\\label{chart:good-windows}\n q(2S_i)\\le K_0q(a_0S_i).$$ One can choose $s_i\\in[5S_i/4,7S_i/4]$ so that $(\\log q)'(s_i)\\to0$.       "explanation": "The volume clock has monotone positive speed and controlled lower asymptotic growth. Compact metric decay and a delayed lower Ricci bound permit selection of windows with uniformly controlled rescaled geometry and positive Ricci pinching at the center.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-shrinking-charts",
        "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large volume clock $s$, there is a holomorphic local biholomorphism $\\Phi_s:B_{\\mathbb C^n}(r_0)\\to M$ centered at $o$, with unitary center derivative for the metric at clock $s$, and $$\\label{chart:uniform-chart-metric}\n C^{-1}g_{\\rm eucl}\\le\\Phi_s^*h_{t(s)}\\le Cg_{\\rm eucl}.$$ For fixed $0<r_1<r_0$, chosen sufficiently small, the transition germs $F_{s,u}=\\Phi_u^{-1}\\Phi_s$, $u\\ge s$, extend to nonsingular holomorphic maps on $B(r_1)$ with a common bound. They fix $0$, compose as germs, and satisfy $$\\label{chart:center-transitions}\n \\|F_{s,u}'(0)\\|\\le1,\\qquad\n |\\det F_{s,u}'(0)|=e^{-(u-s)/2},\\qquad\n \\|F_{s,u}'(0)^{-1}\\|\\le e^{(u-s)/2}.$$ There are coherent inverse branches $f_s$ on neighborhoods of any fixed compact exhaustion set for all sufficiently large $s$, satisfying $f_s(o)=0$ and $\\Phi_sf_s=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Id}}$. A constant $\\sigma>0$, common to all compact sets, satisfies $$\\label{chart:common-exponent}\n \\sup_K|f_s|\\le e^{-\\sigma s}\n \\quad\\text{for every fixed compact }K\n \\text{ and all sufficiently large }s.$$ For every fixed jet order $d$ there is $C_d$ such that $$\\label{chart:transition-jets}\n |J_dF_{s,u}|\\le C_d\\min\\{1,e^{C_ds-\\sigma u}\\}\n \\qquad(u\\ge s\\text{ sufficiently large}).$$ There are $c,\\gamma_0>0$ such that, for each fixed $0<\\gamma\\le\\gamma_0$, one has $$\\label{chart:transition-small-balls}\n \\sup_{|z|\\le e^{-\\gamma s}}|F_{s,u}(z)|\n \\le e^{-c\\gamma u}\n \\qquad\\text{for every }u\\ge s\\ge s_\\gamma.$$ The constants in the last two estimates are independent of the later endpoint $u$; the starting threshold in <a href=\"#chart:common-exponent\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"chart:common-exponent\">[chart:common-exponent]</a> may depend on $K$.",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-shrinking-charts",
          "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large volume clock $s$, there is a holomorphic local biholomorphism $\\Phi_s:B_{\\mathbb C^n}(r_0)\\to M$ centered at $o$, with unitary center derivative for the metric at clock $s$, and $$\\label{chart:uniform-chart-metric}\n C^{-1}g_{\\rm eucl}\\le\\Phi_s^*h_{t(s)}\\le Cg_{\\rm eucl}.$$ For fixed $0<r_1<r_0$, chosen sufficiently small, the transition germs $F_{s,u}=\\Phi_u^{-1}\\Phi_s$, $u\\ge s$, extend to nonsingular holomorphic maps on $B(r_1)$ with a common bound. They fix $0$, compose as germs, and satisfy $$\\label{chart:center-transitions}\n \\|F_{s,u}'(0)\\|\\le1,\\qquad\n |\\det F_{s,u}'(0)|=e^{-(u-s)/2},\\qquad\n \\|F_{s,u}'(0)^{-1}\\|\\le e^{(u-s)/2}.$$ There are coherent inverse branches $f_s$ on neighborhoods of any fixed compact exhaustion set for all sufficiently large $s$, satisfying $f_s(o)=0$ and $\\Phi_sf_s=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Id}}$. A constant $\\sigma>0$, common to all compact sets, satisfies $$\\label{chart:common-exponent}\n \\sup_K|f_s|\\le e^{-\\sigma s}\n \\quad\\text{for every fixed compact }K\n \\text{ and all sufficiently large }s.$$ For every fixed jet order $d$ there is $C_d$ such that $$\\label{chart:transition-jets}\n |J_dF_{s,u}|\\le C_d\\min\\{1,e^{C_ds-\\sigma u}\\}\n \\qquad(u\\ge s\\text{ sufficiently large}).$$ There are $c,\\gamma_0>0$ such that, for each fixed $0<\\gamma\\le\\gamma_0$, one has $$\\label{chart:transition-small-balls}\n \\sup_{|z|\\le e^{-\\gamma s}}|F_{s,u}(z)|\n \\le e^{-c\\gamma u}\n \\qquad\\text{for every }u\\ge s\\ge s_\\gamma.$$ The constants in the last two estimates are independent of the later endpoint $u$; the starting threshold in <a href=\"#chart:common-exponent\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"chart:common-exponent\">[chart:common-exponent]</a> may depend on $K$.",

      "exact_statement": "For every sufficiently large volume clock $s$, there is a holomorphic local biholomorphism $\\Phi_s:B_{\\mathbb C^n}(r_0)\\to M$ centered at $o$, with unitary center derivative for the metric at clock $s$, and $$\\label{chart:uniform-chart-metric}\n C^{-1}g_{\\rm eucl}\\le\\Phi_s^*h_{t(s)}\\le Cg_{\\rm eucl}.$$ For fixed $0<r_1<r_0$, chosen sufficiently small, the transition germs $F_{s,u}=\\Phi_u^{-1}\\Phi_s$, $u\\ge s$, extend to nonsingular holomorphic maps on $B(r_1)$ with a common bound.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-polynomial-models",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $d\\ge2$ and a macro width $0<h<1$. After starting at a sufficiently late macro endpoint, there is a grid $s_k\\to\\infty$ with macro endpoints $S_j=e^{hj}$ and the following properties. Each macro interval is either one bad step or is divided into regular steps of length comparable to $\\sqrt{S_j}$. Thus, eventually, $$c_h\\sqrt{s_k}\\le\\Delta s_k:=s_{k+1}-s_k\n \\le(e^h-1)s_k.\n \\label{eq:dyn:grid-size}$$ For $F_k=F_{s_k,s_{k+1}}$ there are polynomial local coordinate changes $H_k$ and polynomial automorphisms $G_k$ of $\\mathbb C^n$ satisfying $$J_d(H_{k+1}\\circ F_k)=J_d(G_k\\circ H_k).\n \\label{eq:dyn:jet-conjugacy}$$ The degrees of $H_k,G_k,G_k^{-1}$ are bounded independently of $k$. The coefficients of $H_k$ and its inverse jet are $\\exp(o(s_k))$; $H_k'(0)$ and its inverse have polynomial bounds in $s_k$. For a constant $C_d$ depending only on the fixed jet order and dimension, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\lVert G_k\\rVert_{\\mathrm{coeff}}+\\lVert G_k^{-1}\\rVert_{\\mathrm{coeff}}\n &\\le \\exp\\bigl(C_d\\Delta s_k+o(s_k)\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:dyn:coefficient-bound}\\\\\n \\lVert G_k'(0)^{-1}\\rVert\n &\\le s_k^{C}\\exp(\\Delta s_k/2).\n \\label{eq:dyn:center-inverse}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, the coefficient $C_d$ of $\\Delta s_k$ is independent of $h$. Finally, for some finite $D$, $$\\deg G_{0,k}\\le D s_k\n \\quad\\text{on an unbounded subsequence of macro endpoints.}\n \\label{eq:dyn:forward-degree}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-polynomial-models",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $d\\ge2$ and a macro width $0<h<1$. After starting at a sufficiently late macro endpoint, there is a grid $s_k\\to\\infty$ with macro endpoints $S_j=e^{hj}$ and the following properties. Each macro interval is either one bad step or is divided into regular steps of length comparable to $\\sqrt{S_j}$. Thus, eventually, $$c_h\\sqrt{s_k}\\le\\Delta s_k:=s_{k+1}-s_k\n \\le(e^h-1)s_k.\n \\label{eq:dyn:grid-size}$$ For $F_k=F_{s_k,s_{k+1}}$ there are polynomial local coordinate changes $H_k$ and polynomial automorphisms $G_k$ of $\\mathbb C^n$ satisfying $$J_d(H_{k+1}\\circ F_k)=J_d(G_k\\circ H_k).\n \\label{eq:dyn:jet-conjugacy}$$ The degrees of $H_k,G_k,G_k^{-1}$ are bounded independently of $k$. The coefficients of $H_k$ and its inverse jet are $\\exp(o(s_k))$; $H_k'(0)$ and its inverse have polynomial bounds in $s_k$. For a constant $C_d$ depending only on the fixed jet order and dimension, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\lVert G_k\\rVert_{\\mathrm{coeff}}+\\lVert G_k^{-1}\\rVert_{\\mathrm{coeff}}\n &\\le \\exp\\bigl(C_d\\Delta s_k+o(s_k)\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:dyn:coefficient-bound}\\\\\n \\lVert G_k'(0)^{-1}\\rVert\n &\\le s_k^{C}\\exp(\\Delta s_k/2).\n \\label{eq:dyn:center-inverse}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, the coefficient $C_d$ of $\\Delta s_k$ is independent of $h$. Finally, for some finite $D$, $$\\deg G_{0,k}\\le D s_k\n \\quad\\text{on an unbounded subsequence of macro endpoints.}\n \\label{eq:dyn:forward-degree}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix an integer $d\\ge2$ and a macro width $0<h<1$. After starting at a sufficiently late macro endpoint, there is a grid $s_k\\to\\infty$ with macro endpoints $S_j=e^{hj}$ and the following properties. Each macro interval is either one bad step or is divided into regular steps of length comparable to $\\sqrt{S_j}$. Thus, eventually, $$c_h\\sqrt{s_k}\\le\\Delta s_k:=s_{k+1}-s_k\n \\le(e^h-1)s_k.\n \\label{eq:dyn:grid-size}$$ For $F_k=F_{s_k,s_{k+1}}$ there are polynomial local coordinate changes $H_k$ and polynomial automorphisms $G_k$ of $\\mathbb C^n$ satisfying $$J_d(H_{k+1}\\circ F_k)=J_d(G_k\\circ H_k).\n \\label{eq:dyn:jet-conjugacy}$$ The degrees of $H_k,G_k,G_k^{-1}$ are bounded independently of $k$. The coefficients of $H_k$ and its inverse jet are $\\exp(o(s_k))$; $H_k'(0)$ and its inverse have polynomial bounds in $s_k$. For a constant $C_d$ depending only on the fixed jet order and dimension, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\lVert G_k\\rVert_{\\mathrm{coeff}}+\\lVert G_k^{-1}\\rVert_{\\mathrm{coeff}}\n &\\le \\exp\\bigl(C_d\\Delta s_k+o(s_k)\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:dyn:coefficient-bound}\\\\\n \\lVert G_k'(0)^{-1}\\rVert\n &\\le s_k^{C}\\exp(\\Delta s_k/2).\n \\label{eq:dyn:center-inverse}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, the coefficient $C_d$ of $\\Delta s_k$ is independent of $h$. Finally, for some finite $D$, $$\\deg G_{0,k}\\le D s_k\n \\quad\\text{on an unbounded subsequence of macro endpoints.}\n \\label{eq:dyn:forward-degree}$$",
      "explanation": "A late clock grid supports polynomial coordinate corrections and polynomial automorphisms matching transition jets.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-volume-jets",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed $n,d$, every invertible constant-volume $d$-jet fixing zero is the $d$-jet of a polynomial automorphism of $\\mathbb C^n$. Its degree and inverse degree are bounded by a constant $D_d$. Its coefficients and inverse coefficients are bounded polynomially in the coefficients of the given jet and its inverse linear part. These bounds do not depend on the grid or on $h$.",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-volume-jets",

These bounds do not depend on the grid or on $h$.",

        }

      "exact_statement": "For fixed $n,d$, every invertible constant-volume $d$-jet fixing zero is the $d$-jet of a polynomial automorphism of $\\mathbb C^n$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-linear-alignment",
        "exact_quote": "On every run of regular steps there are unipotent matrices $L_k^+$, with off-diagonal entries only in rows of smaller weight than their columns, such that, on putting $L_k^-=D_k^{-1}L_k^+D_k$, the diagonal step derivative remains $D_k$ and every regular jump becomes $$J_k=L_{k+1}^-U_k(L_k^+)^{-1},\\qquad\n (J_k)_{il}=0\\quad\\hbox{if }w_{k+1,i}<w_{k,l}.\n \\label{eq:dyn:aligned-jump}$$ The matrices $L_k^+$ and their inverses have polynomial bounds in the macro index $j$, while $$\\lVert L_k^- -I\\rVert\n \\le j^C\\exp\\left(-c\\frac{\\sqrt{S_j}}{j^2}\\right).\n \\label{eq:dyn:small-start-change}$$ The estimates are uniform in finite terminal horizons, and hold on an infinite regular run by a coefficientwise limit.",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-linear-alignment",
          "exact_quote": "On every run of regular steps there are unipotent matrices $L_k^+$, with off-diagonal entries only in rows of smaller weight than their columns, such that, on putting $L_k^-=D_k^{-1}L_k^+D_k$, the diagonal step derivative remains $D_k$ and every regular jump becomes $$J_k=L_{k+1}^-U_k(L_k^+)^{-1},\\qquad\n (J_k)_{il}=0\\quad\\hbox{if }w_{k+1,i}<w_{k,l}.\n \\label{eq:dyn:aligned-jump}$$ The matrices $L_k^+$ and their inverses have polynomial bounds in the macro index $j$, while $$\\lVert L_k^- -I\\rVert\n \\le j^C\\exp\\left(-c\\frac{\\sqrt{S_j}}{j^2}\\right).\n \\label{eq:dyn:small-start-change}$$ The estimates are uniform in finite terminal horizons, and hold on an infinite regular run by a coefficientwise limit.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "On every run of regular steps there are unipotent matrices $L_k^+$, with off-diagonal entries only in rows of smaller weight than their columns, such that, on putting $L_k^-=D_k^{-1}L_k^+D_k$, the diagonal step derivative remains $D_k$ and every regular jump becomes $$J_k=L_{k+1}^-U_k(L_k^+)^{-1},\\qquad\n (J_k)_{il}=0\\quad\\hbox{if }w_{k+1,i}<w_{k,l}.\n \\label{eq:dyn:aligned-jump}$$ The matrices $L_k^+$ and their inverses have polynomial bounds in the macro index $j$, while $$\\lVert L_k^- -I\\rVert\n \\le j^C\\exp\\left(-c\\frac{\\sqrt{S_j}}{j^2}\\right).\n \\label{eq:dyn:small-start-change}$$ The estimates are uniform in finite terminal horizons, and hold on an infinite regular run by a coefficientwise limit.",
      "explanation": "Unipotent endpoint changes remove weight-decreasing entries from regular linear jumps while retaining the diagonal step derivative. Backward finite-horizon constructions have uniform bounds and admit coefficientwise limits on infinite runs.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-degree-control",
        "exact_quote": "The flag threshold $M$ can be chosen so that regular macro starts occur arbitrarily late, and the polynomial models constructed above, initialized at any sufficiently late such start, satisfy <a href=\"#eq:dyn:forward-degree\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dyn:forward-degree\">[eq:dyn:forward-degree]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-338.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-degree-control",
          "exact_quote": "The flag threshold $M$ can be chosen so that regular macro starts occur arbitrarily late, and the polynomial models constructed above, initialized at any sufficiently late such start, satisfy <a href=\"#eq:dyn:forward-degree\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dyn:forward-degree\">[eq:dyn:forward-degree]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The flag threshold $M$ can be chosen so that regular macro starts occur arbitrarily late, and the polynomial models constructed above, initialized at any sufficiently late such start, satisfy <a href=\"#eq:dyn:forward-degree\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dyn:forward-degree\">[eq:dyn:forward-degree]</a>.",
      "explanation": "A sufficiently large flag threshold makes completed bad components pay for their degree inflation through clock-speed growth. Bad prefixes have a controlled remaining cost, and regular starts occur arbitrarily late.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Plurisubharmonic abbreviation** ([source](#pm-h-5)): The abbreviation psh means plurisubharmonic.
- **Hermitian order estimate** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For Hermitian forms, $A=O(b)h$ means $-Cbh\leq A\leq Cbh$.
- **Initial distance and base point** ([source](#pm-h-5)): The initial complete metric is $g$, a fixed base point is $o$, and $r(x)=d_g(o,x)$.
- **Dual squared norm** ([source](#sec:discs)): Put $$N(x,\xi,t)=\|\xi\|^2_{h_t^{-1}}.$$
- **Raw disc envelope and regularization** ([source](#var:envelope)): If $y\in Y$ and $0<s<T$, let $v^{\rm raw}(y,s)$ be the infimum of $$\left\langle N\bigl(f(\zeta),s e^{w(\zeta)+\overline{w(\zeta)}}\bigr) \right\rangle, \qquad (f(0),w(0))=(y,0), \label{var:envelope}$$ over holomorphic discs $f:\overline\Delta\to Y$ and $w:\overline\Delta\to\mathbb C$, smooth on the closed unit disc, with $s e^{w+\bar w}<T$ there. Brackets denote normalized circle average.
- **Symplectic Hessian polynomial** ([source](#pm-h-18)): In a frame $e_1,\ldots,e_{2n}$, write $\mathcal J=\sum_{A<B}J^{AB}e_A\wedge e_B$ and define $$\label{disc:p-definition} p(W,\mathcal J)= \sum_{A<B,\,C<D}\overline{J^{AB}}J^{CD} (W_{AC}W_{BD}-W_{AD}W_{BC}).$$
- **Boundary half-Sobolev seminorm** ([source](#disc:half-sobolev)): For a vector or matrix function $a$ on the circle, write $$\label{disc:half-sobolev} D(a)^2=\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z}\|k\|\,\\|a_k\\|_F^2 =\frac1{(2\pi)^2}\int_0^{2\pi}\!\int_0^{2\pi} \frac{\\|a(e^{i(t+\theta)})-a(e^{it})\\|_F^2} {\|e^{i\theta}-1\|^2}\,dt\,d\theta,$$ where $a_k$ are Fourier coefficients and $\\|\cdot\\|_F$ is the Frobenius norm.
- **Determinant-maximizing fiber matrix** ([source](#ell:optimizer)): For each $(x,t)$, let $B(x,t)$ be the feasible matrix maximizing the determinant: $$\label{ell:optimizer} \det B(x,t)=\max\bigl\{\det D: D>0,\ \xi^*D\xi\le v(x,\xi,t)\text{ for every }\xi\bigr\}, \qquad \mathcal L=\log\det B.$$
- **Volume clock and clock speed** ([source](#pm-h-36)): Set $$\label{chart:clocks} \tau=\log t,\qquad s=\rho(o,t),\qquad q(s)=\frac{ds}{d\tau}=tR(o,t).$$
- **Taylor jet notation** ([source](#pm-h-38)): $$ Indeed the radial term is $-1/r^2$, while the linear test field in the $JT$ direction gives $1/r^2$ minus the displayed curvature integral. This is the strictly negative Levi derivative on the radial complex line. This support argument applies also at cut points: smoothly vary the endpoint and use trial paths along the chosen minimizing segment. Their lengths give a smooth upper support with the same index-form bound. Since $H$ is increasing, it produces a smooth upper test for $v$ at the hypothetical positive maximum, with a negative Levi derivative. A plurisubharmonic function admits no such upper test, whether or not it is continuous. Hence $v\le H$ on the annulus, proving the claimed convexity. A nondecreasing convex function of $\log r\in\mathbb R$ which is bounded above must be constant. Upper semicontinuity implies $\lim_{r\downarrow0}m(r)=v(o)$, so $v$ attains its global maximum at $o$. The plurisubharmonic maximum principle on connected $M$ shows that $v$ is constant. ◻ </details> Write $J_dF$ for the Taylor jet of $F$ through total degree $d$ at zero, and fix a coefficient norm on each finite-dimensional jet space.
- **Subexponential coefficient bound** ([source](#sec:dynamics)): A coefficient bound $\exp(o(s_k))$ means an upper bound $\exp(\eta_k s_k)$ with $\eta_k\to0$; its rate of convergence may depend on parameters already fixed.
- **Forward polynomial compositions** ([source](#sec:dynamics)): For polynomial automorphisms put $G_{k,l}=G_{l-1}\circ\cdots\circ G_k$ and $G_{k,k}=\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}$.
- **Constant-volume jet** ([source](#pm-h-40)): We shall call a jet with Jacobian determinant equal, through degree $d-1$, to its center determinant a *constant-volume jet*.
- **Allowed and forbidden nonlinear monomials** ([source](#eq:dyn:forbidden-gap)): Call a nonlinear monomial $z^\beta$ in component $i$ *allowed* when $\sum_l\beta_lw_{k,l}\le w_{k,i}$, and *forbidden* otherwise.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-initial-psh-growth | \psi | ≤ | $C(1+r)$ | The growth constant may depend on the initial geometry and fixed center; properness of this function is not asserted. |
| q-initial-ricci-potential-growth | \Xi | ≤ | $C(1+r^{3/2})$ | The constant belongs to the fixed initial geometry. |
| q-initial-average-scalar-curvature | \frac{1}{\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}B(o,L)}\int_{B(o,L)}R_g\,dV_g | ≤ | $C/L$ | The constant is not asserted uniform over all centers. |
| q-flow-average-volume-loss | \sup_{0\le t\le T}\frac{1}{\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_gB_g(o,L)}\int_{B_g(o,L)}\rho(x,t)\,dV_g(x) | ≤ | $C_TL$ | The finite-time constant may depend on the horizon and initial geometry; the radius satisfies the displayed source restriction. |
| q-spectral-factor-supremum | \\|H\\|_{\infty,\mathrm{op}} | ≤ | $\sqrt M$ | The bound uses only the upper boundary spectral bound. |
| q-inverse-spectral-factor-supremum | \\|H^{-1}\\|_{\infty,\mathrm{op}} | ≤ | $m^{-1/2}$ | The bound uses only the positive lower boundary spectral bound. |
| q-spectral-factor-seminorm | D(H)^2 | ≤ | $\frac{M}{2m^2}D(L)^2$ | The displayed prefactor depends only on the boundary spectral bounds and is independent of higher derivatives. |
| q-inverse-factor-seminorm | D(H^{-1})^2 | ≤ | $\frac1{2m^3}D(L)^2$ | The prefactor depends on the positive lower boundary spectral bound, independently of higher derivatives. |
| q-ellipsoid-volume-gap | F_0 | ≤ | $\rho$ | The gap is nonnegative and bounded by logarithmic volume loss on the fixed finite horizon. |
| q-clock-speed-liminf | \liminf_{s\to\infty}\frac{q(s)}s | ≤ | $2$ | This is a lower asymptotic growth bound and does not give an all-time upper bound for the ratio. |
| q-static-singular-length-distortion | B | ≤ | $C(1+A)^C$ | The constants depend on the compact center region, chart radius, pullback lower bound, and initial geometry, independently of total distortion. |
| q-transition-determinant-loss | \|\det F_{s,u}'(0)\| | = | $e^{-(u-s)/2}$ | The identity uses the volume-clock difference and holds at the chart center. |
| q-common-compact-contraction | \sup_K\|f_s\| | ≤ | $e^{-\sigma s}$ | The exponent is common to all fixed compact sets; the starting threshold may depend on the compact set. |
| q-subsequential-forward-degree | \deg G_{0,k} | ≤ | $D s_k$ | The estimate holds on an unbounded subsequence of macro endpoints after a sufficiently late start. |
| q-orbit-defect-decay | \lvert r_k\rvert | ≤ | $e^{-10s_k}$ | The jet order is fixed before the macro width; all remaining fixed-parameter costs are absorbed by a sufficiently late start. |
| q-corrected-coordinate-shadow | \lvert\xi_k-\zeta_k\rvert | ≤ | $e^{-6s_k}$ | The inverse evaluations stay within the established tubes; compact-regime thresholds may depend on the compact set. |

There are finitely many unit columns $\\xi_i$ and positive numbers $\\alpha_i$ such that $$\\label{ell:contact-resolution}\n v(x,\\xi_i,t)=1,\\qquad\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\alpha_i\\xi_i\\xi_i^*=I,\n \\qquad \\sum_{i=1}^m\\alpha_i=n.$$ The largest weight can be made arbitrarily small by repeating columns.",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-ricci-windows",
          "exact_quote": "The function $q$ is nondecreasing, $q\\ge c>0$ for large $s$, and $$\\label{chart:q-growth}\n s\\mathrel{%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F6}%\n  \\longrightarrow%\n  \\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}%\n}\\infty,\\qquad\n q\\le Ce^\\tau,\\qquad\n \\liminf_{s\\to\\infty}\\frac{q(s)}s\\le2.$$ Uniformly on every fixed compact set, $\\rho(x,t)/s\\to1$, and there is $c_K>0$ such that, eventually on that compact set, $$\\label{chart:metric-decay}\n e^{-2s}g\\le h_t\\le e^{-c_Ks}g.$$ There are fixed $a_0\\in(0,1)$ and $b_0>0$ such that the least eigenvalue at $o$ of $t\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}(h_t)$ relative to $h_t$ satisfies $$\\label{chart:ricci-lower}\n \\lambda_{\\min}(s)\\ge b_0q(a_0s)\n \\quad\\text{for all sufficiently large }s.$$ Moreover there are $S_i\\to\\infty$ and $K_0<\\infty$ with $$\\label{chart:good-windows}\n q(2S_i)\\le K_0q(a_0S_i).$$ One can choose $s_i\\in[5S_i/4,7S_i/4]$ so that $(\\log q)'(s_i)\\to0$. If $t_i$ corresponds to $s_i$ and $R_i=R(o,t_i)$, the rescaled flows $$\\label{chart:rescaled-flows}\n g_i(z)=R_i h_{t_i+z/R_i}$$ have uniform curvature and derivative bounds on each fixed-radius moving ball about $o$ in a fixed small two-sided time interval. At $(o,0)$ their scalar curvature is one and their Ricci eigenvalues have a fixed positive lower bound.",

Let $A$ and $B$ be the least and greatest singular lengths of $d\\Phi(0)$, using the Euclidean metric in the domain and $g$ in the target.
