        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-shrinking-charts",
          "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large volume clock $s$, there is a holomorphic local biholomorphism $\\Phi_s:B_{\\mathbb C^n}(r_0)\\to M$ centered at $o$, with unitary center derivative for the metric at clock $s$, and $$\\label{chart:uniform-chart-metric}\n C^{-1}g_{\\rm eucl}\\le\\Phi_s^*h_{t(s)}\\le Cg_{\\rm eucl}.$$ For fixed $0<r_1<r_0$, chosen sufficiently small, the transition germs $F_{s,u}=\\Phi_u^{-1}\\Phi_s$, $u\\ge s$, extend to nonsingular holomorphic maps on $B(r_1)$ with a common bound. They fix $0$, compose as germs, and satisfy $$\\label{chart:center-transitions}\n \\|F_{s,u}'(0)\\|\\le1,\\qquad\n |\\det F_{s,u}'(0)|=e^{-(u-s)/2},\\qquad\n \\|F_{s,u}'(0)^{-1}\\|\\le e^{(u-s)/2}.$$ There are coherent inverse branches $f_s$ on neighborhoods of any fixed compact exhaustion set for all sufficiently large $s$, satisfying $f_s(o)=0$ and $\\Phi_sf_s=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Id}}$. A constant $\\sigma>0$, common to all compact sets, satisfies $$\\label{chart:common-exponent}\n \\sup_K|f_s|\\le e^{-\\sigma s}\n \\quad\\text{for every fixed compact }K\n \\text{ and all sufficiently large }s.$$ For every fixed jet order $d$ there is $C_d$ such that $$\\label{chart:transition-jets}\n |J_dF_{s,u}|\\le C_d\\min\\{1,e^{C_ds-\\sigma u}\\}\n \\qquad(u\\ge s\\text{ sufficiently large}).$$ There are $c,\\gamma_0>0$ such that, for each fixed $0<\\gamma\\le\\gamma_0$, one has $$\\label{chart:transition-small-balls}\n \\sup_{|z|\\le e^{-\\gamma s}}|F_{s,u}(z)|\n \\le e^{-c\\gamma u}\n \\qquad\\text{for every }u\\ge s\\ge s_\\gamma.$$ The constants in the last two estimates are independent of the later endpoint $u$; the starting threshold in <a href=\"#chart:common-exponent\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"chart:common-exponent\">[chart:common-exponent]</a> may depend on $K$.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-initial-analytic-data** [claim](#stmt-prop-initial-data) / [proof](#pm-h-7): Use Busemann comparison, weighted holomorphic interpolation, canonical sections, heat smoothing, and cutoff integration. -> Interpolation families, canonical potentials, controlled exhaustion, and average curvature bounds.
- **step-hodge-heat** [claim](#stmt-lem-hodge-heat) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hodge-heat): Pass cone preservation and scalar trace commutation from domain heat operators to the complete semigroup, then use off-diagonal decay and complete Kähler commutation. -> Heat order preservation and scalar-potential commutation.
- **step-localized-global-flow** [claim](#stmt-thm-localized-flow) / [proof](#pm-h-13): Construct complete Hermitian flows on exhaustion domains, establish potential and eigenvalue barriers, and extract smooth diagonal limits. -> Global Kähler–Ricci flow with potential identities, volume-loss estimates, and proper supercaloric barriers.
- **step-adapted-integrable-cutoff** [claim](#stmt-flow-cutoff-lemma) / [proof](#proof-stmt-flow-cutoff-lemma): Heat-correct the metric deficit, glue corrections over spatial scales, and exponentiate the correction inside a polynomial weight. -> Integrable comparison weight with evolving-metric Hessian control.
- **step-smooth-nonlinear-majorant** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-majorant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-majorant-2): Solve compatible compact-domain nonlinear Dirichlet problems using barriers and local continuation, then exhaust the manifold. -> Smooth majorant solving the nonlinear equality with zero initial values.
- **step-scalar-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-scalar-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-majorant): Construct minimal source potentials and exterior tails; apply weighted mass identities, the cofactor bound, and Gronwall comparison. -> Minimality of volume loss, vanishing exterior tails, and nonlinear scalar comparison without positive-time completeness.
- **step-symplectic-evolution** [claim](#stmt-lem-symplectic-evolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-symplectic-evolution): Compute Hessian blocks and dual-metric evolution in Kähler-normal coordinates, retaining the tangent–cotangent transpose. -> Symplectic norm evolution and base-addition identity.
- **step-extra-integrability** [claim](#stmt-lem-extra-integrability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-extra-integrability): Use weighted bundle-valued correction estimates and finite-jet convergence, followed by the Noetherian module and Krull intersection argument. -> A positive extra integrability exponent near the puncture.
- **step-psh-lower-anchor** [claim](#stmt-lem-psh-compactness-bridge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-psh-compactness-bridge): Propagate local integral bounds from moving lower anchors along overlapping coordinate balls and apply subharmonic compactness. -> Subsequential compactness or collapse, with collapse excluded by a lower anchor.
- **step-disc-compactness** [claim](#stmt-prop-disc-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-psh-compactness-bridge-2): Combine compact energy anchors, inverse-Jacobian integrability, Morrey control, puncture removal, and global spanning-field evaluations. -> Compact containment and interior holomorphic subsequential convergence of base and cotangent discs.
- **step-boundary-factorization** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-factorization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-factorization): Construct a factor from the wandering subspace of a weighted Hardy shift, estimate symmetric differences, and bootstrap boundary regularity. -> Holomorphic invertible factor with supremum and boundary seminorm bounds.
- **step-normalized-disc-frames** [claim](#stmt-lem-disc-frames) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-factorization-2): Trivialize the pulled-back tangent bundle and factor its boundary Gram matrix; use Green's formula and the canonical bivector to control area and center frames. -> Boundary-normalized frames with boundary-dependent symplectic and center bounds.
- **step-disc-deformation** [claim](#stmt-lem-disc-deformation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-disc-deformation): Realize weighted holomorphic first fields, justify uniform Taylor expansions at fixed localization parameters, and express mixed energy derivatives through boundary integrals. -> Deformation inequality with a boundary-dependent penalty coefficient.
- **step-symplectic-schur** [claim](#stmt-lem-symplectic-schur) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-symplectic-schur): Complete the vertical square and retain the additional nonnegative vertical bivector term created by the shear. -> Positive Schur majorant and symplectic trace-cost bound.
- **step-disc-envelope-inequality** [claim](#stmt-prop-disc-inequality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-symplectic-schur-2): Remove the boundary negative part with disc-dependent Fejér tests, factor positive replacements, vary the clock backward, and pass to Schur limits in the prescribed order. -> Viscosity lower bound for the disc envelope with positive vertical tests.
- **step-ellipsoid-optimizer** [claim](#stmt-ell-optimizer-contacts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ell-optimizer-contacts): Use compact feasible matrix sets, strict log-determinant concavity, lower semicontinuity, and finite-dimensional cone separation. -> Unique optimizer, bounded scalar volume gap, and finite contact resolution.
- **step-ellipsoid-scalar-inequality** [claim](#stmt-prop-ellipsoid-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ellipsoid-comparison): Build a common-base product support, select simultaneous Alexandrov contacts by inf-convolution, transfer lower tests, and aggregate positive Schur majorants. -> Lower viscosity inequality for the optimizer log-determinant.
- **step-joint-positivity** [claim](#stmt-thm-joint-positivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-joint-positivity): Apply nonlinear scalar comparison to the determinant gap, identify the optimizer with the cometric, and recover submean inequalities for the raw envelope. -> Equality of envelope and norm, and joint cotangent plurisubharmonicity.
- **step-harnack-completeness** [claim](#stmt-prop-harnack-completeness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-harnack-completeness): Read curvature blocks from joint positivity, integrate the scalar Harnack inequality along approximate distance paths, and control exit lengths using index forms. -> Positive-time completeness, Ricci positivity, Harnack estimates, and compact power contraction.
- **step-uniform-local-charts** [claim](#stmt-chart-local-charts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-chart-local-charts): Use moving-ball curvature estimates, pullbacks by exponential maps, smooth complex-structure compactness, and small holomorphic-coordinate corrections. -> Uniform immersed charts and local smooth compactness for rescaled flows.
- **step-static-distortion** [claim](#stmt-lem-static-distortion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-static-distortion): Construct a strict moving-ball plurisubharmonic weight and extend a local function detecting maximal singular length by a weighted holomorphic correction. -> Polynomial relation between greatest and least initial-metric singular lengths.
- **step-volume-clock-windows** [claim](#stmt-prop-ricci-windows) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ricci-windows): Combine Harnack control, static distortion, convex logarithmic covector norms, and monotone-clock growth to select pinched rescaled windows. -> Compact exponential metric decay, delayed Ricci lower bound, and good rescaled windows.
- **step-contract-loops** [claim](#stmt-prop-simple-connectivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-simple-connectivity): Analyze equality in a limiting Harnack inequality, prove strict real scalar-curvature concavity on intrinsic balls, and contract loops by its gradient flow. -> Simple connectivity of the manifold.
- **step-psh-liouville** [claim](#stmt-chart-psh-liouville) / [proof](#proof-stmt-chart-psh-liouville): Compare compact-ball maxima with logarithmic chords using strict radial Levi upper supports and apply the plurisubharmonic maximum principle. -> Constancy of every bounded-above plurisubharmonic function, without continuity.
- **step-coherent-shrinking-charts** [claim](#stmt-prop-shrinking-charts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-shrinking-charts): Lift short paths and homotopies, patch inverse germs, apply plurisubharmonic compactness and Liouville to obtain a common exponent, then use Cauchy and three-circle estimates. -> Coherent inverse branches and endpoint-uniform transition estimates.
- **step-volume-jet-realization** [claim](#stmt-lem-volume-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-volume-jets): Decompose homogeneous divergence-free discrepancies into a fixed finite spanning family of shear fields and correct successive jet layers. -> Polynomial automorphism realization with bounded degree and polynomial coefficient control.
- **step-linear-weight-alignment** [claim](#stmt-lem-linear-alignment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-linear-alignment): Use endpoint rate ordering and nested graph extensions in a backward finite-horizon construction, then take coefficientwise limits. -> Weight-compatible regular linear jumps and small start changes.
- **step-forward-degree-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-degree-control) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-degree-control): Select disjoint flag intervals to charge completed bad-component degree costs to monotone rate growth, and handle unfinished prefixes separately. -> Arbitrarily late regular starts and a subsequential linear forward-degree bound.
- **step-polynomial-models** [claim](#stmt-prop-polynomial-models) / [proof](#pm-h-44): Normalize Jacobian jets by a convergent cocycle series, solve forbidden homological layers backward on regular runs, and realize corrected jets at bad steps. -> Polynomial jet conjugacies with coefficient bounds and subsequential forward-degree control.
- **step-backward-coordinate-limit** [claim](#stmt-lem-unif-backward-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-unif-backward-limit): Choose decay parameters before the macro width, estimate orbit defects, and close a simultaneous backward error-and-tube induction. -> Locally uniform holomorphic corrected coordinates with exact model intertwining.
- **step-global-open-image-map** [claim](#stmt-prop-global-coordinates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-global-coordinates): Patch corrected limits over the exhaustion, prove quantitative point and derivative separation, and obtain inverse-ball inclusion by a local contraction argument. -> Injective nonsingular global holomorphic coordinates onto an open image, with forward smallness and inverse-ball inclusion.
- **step-surjective-uniformization** [claim](#stmt-thm-uniformization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-global-coordinates-2): Assume a finite maximal centered-ball radius, compare polynomial growth across nearby radii on the degree-controlled subsequence, and contradict inverse-ball inclusion. -> The open image is all of complex Euclidean space, completing the biholomorphism.

      "reason": "The argument uses infima over holomorphic discs and successive minimizing-sequence and penalty limits; it does not assert existence of a minimizing disc.",
      "step_id": "step-disc-envelope-inequality"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-linear-alignment",
          "exact_quote": "A diagonal subsequence of finite-horizon matrices proves the assertion on infinite runs; the zero conditions and all bounds are closed under that limit.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "An infinite regular run is handled by a diagonal subsequence of finite-horizon matrix constructions.",
      "step_id": "step-linear-weight-alignment"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-global-coordinates",
          "exact_quote": "The locally uniform limit on the exhaustion $$\\Psi(x)=\\lim_{l\\to\\infty}G_{0,l}^{-1}H_lf_{s_l}(x)\n \\label{eq:unif:global-map}$$ defines an injective holomorphic map $M\\to\\mathbb C^n$ with $\\Psi(o)=0$. Its image $\\Omega$ is open, and $\\Psi:M\\to\\Omega$ is a biholomorphism. Moreover, on every compact $K\\subset\\Omega$, $$\\sup_K\\lvert G_{0,k}\\rvert\\le e^{-(\\sigma/2)s_k}\n \\quad\\hbox{eventually},\n \\label{eq:unif:forward-smallness}$$ and, at every sufficiently large $k$, $$G_{0,k}^{-1}\\bigl(B(e^{-\\gamma's_k})\\bigr)\\subset\\Omega.\n \\label{eq:unif:inverse-ball-inclusion}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The global coordinate map is a locally uniform infinite backward-iteration limit, with sufficiently late thresholds rather than a finite stopping certificate.",
      "step_id": "step-global-open-image-map"
    }
  ],
  "pm_id": "PM-338.1",
  "schema": "pm.roadmap.v2",
  "steps": [
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-initial-data",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-hodge-heat"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-initial-data",
          "exact_quote": "The following objects and estimates are available.*\n\n1.  *There is a smooth strictly plurisubharmonic function $\\psi$ with $\\psi\\le C(1+r)$.*\n\n2.  *Holomorphic functions and holomorphic tangent fields interpolate arbitrary finite jets at a finite set of points. There are finitely many holomorphic functions defining an injective immersion $F:M\\mathrel{%\n      \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F6}%\n      \\longrightarrow%\n      \\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}%\n    }\\mathbb C^N$, and finitely many global holomorphic tangent fields spanning $T^{1,0}M$ at every point.*\n\n\n3.  *At every point $x$ there is a global holomorphic canonical section $S$ with $S(x)\\ne0$ and $\\log|S|_g^2\\le C_S(1+r)$. There is also a smooth function $\\Xi$ such that $$\\label{prelim:xi}\n          \\partial\\bar\\partial\\Xi\\ge\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}(g),\\qquad \\Xi\\le C(1+r^{3/2}).$$*\n\n\n4.  *There is a smooth exhaustion $u\\ge1$ such that $$\\label{prelim:exhaustion}\n          C^{-1}(1+r)\\le u\\le C(1+r),\\qquad\n          |\\partial u|_g\\le C,\\qquad |\\partial\\bar\\partial u|_g\\le C/u.$$*\n\n\n5.  *If $S(o)\\ne0$ is chosen as in <span class=\"upright\">(iii)</span>, then, for $L\\ge1$, $$\\label{prelim:averages}\n          \\frac{1}{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Vol}}B(o,L)}\\int_{B(o,L)}\n               \\bigl|\\log|S|_g^2\\bigr|\\,dV_g\\le CL,\n          \\qquad\n          \\frac{1}{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Vol}}B(o,L)}\\int_{B(o,L)}R_g\\,dV_g\\le C/L.$$*\n\n*Neither $F$ nor $\\psi$ is asserted to be proper. The exhaustion in <span class=\"upright\">(iv)</span> has a two-sided complex Hessian bound.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-initial-analytic-data",
      "inputs": [
        "Complete initial Kähler metric",
        "Strictly positive holomorphic bisectional curvature"
      ],
      "method": "Use Busemann comparison, weighted holomorphic interpolation, canonical sections, heat smoothing, and cutoff integration.",
      "output": "Interpolation families, canonical potentials, controlled exhaustion, and average curvature bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "pm-h-7"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-hodge-heat",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-hodge-heat",
          "exact_quote": "For bounded real $(1,1)$-forms, Hodge heat is well defined by cutoff limits of the complete $L^2$ semigroup. It preserves order and obeys $$-Cg\\le A\\le Cg\\quad\\mathrel{%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F9}%\n  \\Longrightarrow%\n  \\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}%\n}\\quad\n -Cg\\le\\mathcal H_aA\\le Cg,\n \\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_g\\mathcal H_aA=H_a\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_g A.$$ If $A$ is also smooth and closed, then $$\\label{prelim:hodge-identity}\n \\mathcal H_aA\n   =A+\\partial\\bar\\partial\\int_0^aH_b\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_g A\\,db.$$ If a Lipschitz function $f$ is constant off a compact set and $\\partial\\bar\\partial f\\le Cg$ as currents, then $$\\label{prelim:hessian-preservation}\n \\partial\\bar\\partial H_af\\le Cg\\qquad(a>0).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-hodge-heat",
      "inputs": [
        "Initial complete metric",
        "Bounded real forms",
        "Closedness or current Hessian assumptions where required"
      ],
      "method": "Pass cone preservation and scalar trace commutation from domain heat operators to the complete semigroup, then use off-diagonal decay and complete Kähler commutation.",
      "output": "Heat order preservation and scalar-potential commutation.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-hodge-heat"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-thm-localized-flow",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-initial-analytic-data"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-localized-flow",
          "exact_quote": "There is a smooth Kähler–Ricci flow $h_t$ on $M$, for $0\\le t<\\infty$, with $h_0=g$. There are smooth real functions $U,P$ and $\\rho$ such that $$\\label{flow:identities}\n \\begin{gathered}\n 0<h_t\\le g,\\qquad\n h_t=g-t\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}(g)+\\partial\\bar\\partial U,\\qquad U(\\cdot,0)=0,\\\\\n P=U_t=\\log\\frac{\\det h_t}{\\det g},\\qquad\n \\rho=-P\\ge0,\\qquad R_{h_t}=-P_t\\ge0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ For each finite $T$, there is $C_T$ such that $$\\label{flow:rho-average}\n \\sup_{0\\le t\\le T}\n \\frac{1}{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Vol}}_gB_g(o,L)}\\int_{B_g(o,L)}\\rho(x,t)\\,dV_g(x)\n \\le C_TL\\qquad(L\\ge1).$$ At every fixed $x$, $\\rho(x,t)\\le C_x(1+t)$ for all $t\\ge0$. Furthermore, on each finite time interval there is a smooth positive function $Q$ and a constant $c>0$ with $$\\label{flow:proper-supercaloric}\n Q\\ge c(1+r^2),\\qquad\n (\\partial_t-\\Delta_{h_t})Q\\ge c\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{h_t}g.$$ Smoothness at $t=0$ means smoothness with all one-sided time derivatives.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-localized-global-flow",
      "inputs": [
        "step-initial-analytic-data"
      ],
      "method": "Construct complete Hermitian flows on exhaustion domains, establish potential and eigenvalue barriers, and extract smooth diagonal limits.",
      "output": "Global Kähler–Ricci flow with potential identities, volume-loss estimates, and proper supercaloric barriers.",
      "proof_anchor": "pm-h-13"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-flow-cutoff-lemma",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-localized-global-flow",
        "step-hodge-heat"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-flow-cutoff-lemma",
          "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large fixed integer $k$, there is a positive function $\\chi(x,t)$ on $M\\times[0,T]$, smooth in space and continuously differentiable in time one-sided at the initial surface, such that $$\\label{flow:cutoff-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n C_T^{-1}u^{-k}\\le\\chi\\le C_Tu^{-k},\\qquad\n |\\partial\\chi|_g\\le C_T\\chi/u,\\\\\n \\partial\\bar\\partial\\chi\\le C_T\\chi h_t,\n \\qquad \\partial_t\\chi\\le C_T(1+t^{\\beta-1})\\chi\n \\quad(t>0).\n \\end{gathered}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-adapted-integrable-cutoff",
      "inputs": [
        "step-localized-global-flow",
        "step-hodge-heat",
        "Controlled initial exhaustion"
      ],
      "method": "Heat-correct the metric deficit, glue corrections over spatial scales, and exponentiate the correction inside a polynomial weight.",
      "output": "Integrable comparison weight with evolving-metric Hessian control.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-flow-cutoff-lemma"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-majorant",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-localized-global-flow"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-majorant",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $T>0$ and let $v$ be upper semicontinuous, bounded on compact spacetime sets, and satisfy $0\\le v\\le\\rho$ on $M\\times[0,T]$. Suppose that $v$ satisfies <a href=\"#flow:nonlinear-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flow:nonlinear-comparison\">[flow:nonlinear-comparison]</a> in the upper viscosity sense for $0<t<T$. There is a function $w$, smooth on $M\\times(0,T)$ and continuous on $M\\times[0,T)$, such that $$v\\le w\\le\\rho,\\qquad\n w_t=e^{-w}\\Delta_{h_t}w+(1-e^{-w})R_{h_t},\\qquad\n w(\\cdot,0)=0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-smooth-nonlinear-majorant",
      "inputs": [
        "step-localized-global-flow",
        "Locally bounded viscosity subsolution dominated by logarithmic volume loss"
      ],
      "method": "Solve compatible compact-domain nonlinear Dirichlet problems using barriers and local continuation, then exhaust the manifold.",
      "output": "Smooth majorant solving the nonlinear equality with zero initial values.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-scalar-majorant-2"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-scalar-comparison",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-adapted-integrable-cutoff",
        "step-smooth-nonlinear-majorant"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-scalar-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a finite $T>0$. The following conclusions hold for the flow of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:localized-flow\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:localized-flow\">4</a>.*\n\n\n\n1.  *The function $\\rho$ is the minimal nonnegative solution with zero initial values of $$\\label{flow:rho-source}\n          (\\partial_t-\\Delta_{h_t})\\rho\n                            =\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{h_t}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}(g).$$ There are nonnegative smooth supercaloric functions $\\rho_j$ on $M\\times[0,T]$ such that $$\\label{flow:rho-tails}\n          0\\le\\rho_j\\le\\rho,\\qquad\n          \\rho_j\\ge\\rho-C_j,\\qquad\n          \\rho_j\\mathrel{%\n      \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F6}%\n      \\longrightarrow%\n      \\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}%\n    }0\n          \\text{ locally uniformly on }M\\times[0,T].$$ Each fixed $\\rho_j$ has all spatial and one-sided time derivatives smooth at $t=0$ and bounded on compact spacetime sets.*\n\n\n2.  *Suppose $v$ is upper semicontinuous, bounded on compact spacetime sets, $0\\le v\\le\\rho$, and satisfies, in the upper viscosity sense on $0<t<T$, $$\\label{flow:nonlinear-comparison}\n          \\partial_tv\\le e^{-v}\\Delta_{h_t}v+(1-e^{-v})R_{h_t}.$$ Then $v=0$ on $M\\times[0,T)$.*\n\n*Neither assertion requires completeness of $h_t$.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-scalar-comparison",
      "inputs": [
        "step-adapted-integrable-cutoff",
        "step-smooth-nonlinear-majorant"
      ],
      "method": "Construct minimal source potentials and exterior tails; apply weighted mass identities, the cofactor bound, and Gronwall comparison.",
      "output": "Minimality of volume loss, vanishing exterior tails, and nonlinear scalar comparison without positive-time completeness.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-scalar-majorant"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-symplectic-evolution",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-localized-global-flow"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-symplectic-evolution",
          "exact_quote": "For the flow of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:localized-flow\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:localized-flow\">4</a>, $$\\label{disc:symplectic-evolution}\n \\partial_tN=p(\\partial\\bar\\partial_YN).$$ For every real smooth base function $d=d(x,t)$, with $t$ held fixed in the spatial Hessian, $$\\label{disc:base-addition}\n p(\\partial\\bar\\partial_Y(N+d))=\\partial_tN+\\Delta_{h_t}d.$$ These identities do not assume that $h_t$ is complete or has nonnegative curvature at positive time.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],

      "inputs": [

        "Canonical cotangent bivector"
      ],
      "method": "Compute Hessian blocks and dual-metric evolution in Kähler-normal coordinates, retaining the tangent–cotangent transpose.",
      "output": "Symplectic norm evolution and base-addition identity.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-symplectic-evolution"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-extra-integrability",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-extra-integrability",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(E,k)$ be a smooth Hermitian holomorphic vector bundle on $\\Delta^*=\\{0<|z|<1\\}$, with Griffiths nonnegative curvature. Suppose that $A:E\\to\\Delta^*\\times\\mathbb C^q$ is holomorphic, pointwise injective, and has bounded operator norm. If $a$ is a holomorphic section with $\\int_{\\Delta^*}|a|_k^2\\,dA<\\infty$, then there are $r_0>0$ and $\\sigma>0$ such that $$\\label{disc:extra-integrability}\n \\int_{0<|z|<r_0}|a|_k^2|z|^{-\\sigma}\\,dA<\\infty.$$ The exponent may depend on the section and the bundle data.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-extra-integrability",
      "inputs": [
        "Griffiths nonnegative bundle on a punctured disc",
        "Bounded injective holomorphic bundle map",
        "Square-integrable holomorphic section"
      ],
      "method": "Use weighted bundle-valued correction estimates and finite-jet convergence, followed by the Noetherian module and Krull intersection argument.",
      "output": "A positive extra integrability exponent near the puncture.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-extra-integrability"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-psh-compactness-bridge",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-psh-compactness-bridge",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega_j$ be increasing open subsets exhausting a connected complex manifold $X$, and let $u_j$ be plurisubharmonic on $\\Omega_j$. Assume that for each compact $K\\subset X$, the functions $u_j$ are uniformly bounded above on $K$ once $K\\subset\\Omega_j$. Every sequence has a subsequence for which either $u_j$ tends to $-\\infty$ locally uniformly from above, or $u_j$ converges in $L^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ to a plurisubharmonic function. A lower bound $u_j(z_j)\\ge-A$ at points $z_j$ in a fixed compact set excludes the first alternative along that sequence. In particular, lower bounds on moving subsets of a fixed compact set of uniformly positive measure suffice. If the $L^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ limit is the constant $c$, then $$\\limsup_j\\sup_K u_j\\le c$$ for every compact $K\\subset X$.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-psh-lower-anchor",
      "inputs": [
        "Locally upper-bounded plurisubharmonic sequence",
        "Connected exhausting domains"
      ],
      "method": "Propagate local integral bounds from moving lower anchors along overlapping coordinate balls and apply subharmonic compactness.",
      "output": "Subsequential compactness or collapse, with collapse excluded by a lower anchor.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-psh-compactness-bridge"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-disc-compactness",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-initial-analytic-data",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-disc-compactness",
          "exact_quote": "For each compact $K\\subset M$ and each $A_0<\\infty$, there is a compact $K'\\subset M$ such that every holomorphic disc $f:\\overline\\Delta\\to M$, smooth up to the circle, satisfying $$f(\\partial\\Delta)\\subset K,\\qquad\n \\int_\\Delta f^*\\omega_g\\le A_0$$ has $f(\\overline\\Delta)\\subset K'$. Every sequence of such discs has a subsequence converging holomorphically on compact subsets of $\\Delta$.*\n\n*The same statements hold for discs in $Y$ with compact boundary in $Y$ and with $\\int_\\Delta(\\pi\\circ f)^*\\omega_g\\le A_0$. The resulting compact may depend on $K$ and $A_0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-disc-compactness",
      "inputs": [
        "step-initial-analytic-data",
        "step-extra-integrability",
        "step-psh-lower-anchor",
        "Compact boundaries and bounded projected area"
      ],
      "method": "Combine compact energy anchors, inverse-Jacobian integrability, Morrey control, puncture removal, and global spanning-field evaluations.",
      "output": "Compact containment and interior holomorphic subsequential convergence of base and cotangent discs.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-psh-compactness-bridge-2"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-factorization",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-factorization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be an $r\\times r$ smooth Hermitian matrix on the circle with $mI\\le L\\le MI$, where $0<m\\le M<\\infty$. There is a holomorphic invertible matrix $H$ on $\\Delta$, smooth with smooth inverse on $\\overline\\Delta$, such that $L=H^*H$ on the circle. It satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|H\\|_{\\infty,\\mathrm{op}}&\\le\\sqrt M,&\n \\|H^{-1}\\|_{\\infty,\\mathrm{op}}&\\le m^{-1/2},\\label{disc:factor-sup}\\\\\n D(H)^2&\\le\\frac{M}{2m^2}D(L)^2,&\n D(H^{-1})^2&\\le\\frac1{2m^3}D(L)^2.\n \\label{disc:factor-seminorm}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular the seminorm constants depend only on the boundary spectral bounds, independently of higher derivatives of $L$.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-boundary-factorization",
      "inputs": [
        "Smooth positive Hermitian boundary matrix",
        "Positive lower and finite upper spectral bounds"
      ],
      "method": "Construct a factor from the wandering subspace of a weighted Hardy shift, estimate symmetric differences, and bootstrap boundary regularity.",
      "output": "Holomorphic invertible factor with supremum and boundary seminorm bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-boundary-factorization"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-disc-frames",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-initial-analytic-data",
        "step-boundary-factorization"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-disc-frames",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f$ be a holomorphic disc in $Y$ extending past the unit circle. There is a holomorphic frame $E_f$ of $f^*TY$, smooth on the closed disc, for which $U=dG\\,E_f$ satisfies $$\\label{disc:frame-unitary}\n U^*U=I\\quad\\hbox{on }\\partial\\Delta,\\qquad\n \\sup_\\Delta\\|U\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\le1.$$ Let $\\mathfrak g:Y\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}(2n,q)$ send $y$ to $dG(T_yY)$, and normalize the Plücker form by $\\omega_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}}=i\\partial\\bar\\partial\\log\\det(V^*V)$ for a local holomorphic frame $V$ of its tautological plane. Then $$\\label{disc:gauss-area}\n D(U)^2=\\frac1{2\\pi}\\int_\\Delta\n                          (\\mathfrak g\\circ f)^*\\omega_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}}.$$ If $f(\\partial\\Delta)\\subset K$ for a fixed compact $K\\subset Y$, the holomorphic component matrix $J_{E_f}$ of $\\mathcal J$ in this frame is bounded throughout $\\Delta$ by a constant depending only on $K$ and $G$. If also $f(0)$ ranges in a compact subset $K_0$ of a fixed coordinate chart, the frame matrix at zero and its inverse are bounded by constants depending only on $K$, $K_0$, $G$ and that chart. These constants do not depend on the disc area or on an interior compact containing its image.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-normalized-disc-frames",
      "inputs": [
        "step-initial-analytic-data",
        "step-boundary-factorization",
        "Disc extending past the circle"
      ],
      "method": "Trivialize the pulled-back tangent bundle and factor its boundary Gram matrix; use Green's formula and the canonical bivector to control area and center frames.",
      "output": "Boundary-normalized frames with boundary-dependent symplectic and center bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-boundary-factorization-2"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-disc-deformation",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-disc-compactness",
        "step-normalized-disc-frames",
        "step-scalar-comparison"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-disc-deformation",
          "exact_quote": "Use the preceding localization, with $j$ sufficiently large and $\\varepsilon>0$ sufficiently small that the minimizing-sequence centers have a common interior margin in $\\mathcal C$. Fix a bound $L<\\infty$ and such a sequence for <a href=\"#var:penalized-functional\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"var:penalized-functional\">[var:penalized-functional]</a>, with energy bounded by $E_0<\\infty$. Let $l_\\nu$ be any holomorphic columns, smooth on the closed disc, such that $$\\|l_\\nu\\|_\\infty\\le L,\\qquad D(l_\\nu)\\le D_0<\\infty.$$ They may depend on the disc. With $\\varphi_{E,c}=E_f(0)^*\\partial\\bar\\partial_Y\\varphi(f(0),s)E_f(0)$, one has $$\\limsup_{\\nu\\to\\infty}\n \\left\\{\n h(0)^2\\varphi_{E,c}(l(0),\\overline{l(0)})\n -\\langle T_b(l,\\bar l)\\rangle\n -C_j(L)\\varepsilon\\bigl(1+\\mathcal E+D(l)^2\\bigr)\n \\right\\}\\le0.\n \\label{var:deformation-estimate}$$ The constant $C_j(L)$ depends only on $L$, the dimension, the chosen integer $a$, the fixed time interval and center cylinder, and smooth coefficient bounds for the metrics, immersion, Gauss map and cutoffs on a fixed neighborhood of the boundary compact $K_j$. It is independent of $\\varepsilon,E_0,D_0$, of the interior compact $K_{j,\\varepsilon}'$, and of the auxiliary loss introduced in the proof. In contrast, the existence radius of the deformations, bounds on their full coefficients, and bounds on Taylor remainders may depend on $j,L,E_0,D_0,K_{j,\\varepsilon}'$, that loss, the interior margin of the centers, and the fixed smooth test $\\varphi$.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-disc-deformation",
      "inputs": [
        "step-disc-compactness",
        "step-normalized-disc-frames",
        "Localized penalized almost minimizers"
      ],
      "method": "Realize weighted holomorphic first fields, justify uniform Taylor expansions at fixed localization parameters, and express mixed energy derivatives through boundary integrals.",
      "output": "Deformation inequality with a boundary-dependent penalty coefficient.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-disc-deformation"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-symplectic-schur",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-symplectic-schur",
          "exact_quote": "In a cotangent vector space with canonical bivector $\\mathcal J=\\sum_i e_i\\wedge f_i$, let $\\mathcal H>0$ be Hermitian, and suppose $\\mathcal H\\ge W$, where the vertical block $C$ of $W$ is positive. Then $$S=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Schur}}(\\mathcal H)\\ge0,\\qquad\n S\\ge\\mathop{\\mathrm{Schur}}(W),\\qquad p(\\mathcal H)\\ge\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(C^{\\mathsf T}S).\n \\label{var:schur-cost}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-symplectic-schur",

    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-disc-inequality",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-disc-deformation",
        "step-boundary-factorization",
        "step-symplectic-evolution",

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-disc-inequality",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that a smooth real function $\\varphi$ touches $v$ from below at $(y_*,s_*)$, where $0<s_*<T$. In holomorphic cotangent coordinates write $$W=\\partial\\bar\\partial_Y\\varphi(y_*,s_*)=\n \\begin{pmatrix} A&B\\\\ B^*&C\\end{pmatrix},\\qquad C>0.$$ There is a Hermitian form $K$ on the horizontal coordinate space such that $$K\\ge0,\\qquad K\\ge A-BC^{-1}B^*,\\qquad\n \\partial_s\\varphi(y_*,s_*)\\ge\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(C^{\\mathsf T}K).\n \\label{var:viscosity-inequality}$$ The transpose is the tangent–cotangent contraction in these coordinates. In particular the statement applies to lower tests translated in a fixed cotangent coordinate chart; its contraction has constant coefficients.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-disc-envelope-inequality",

      "output": "Viscosity lower bound for the disc envelope with positive vertical tests.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-symplectic-schur-2"

          "anchor": "stmt-ell-optimizer-contacts",
          "exact_quote": "The maximum in <a href=\"#ell:optimizer\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ell:optimizer\">[ell:optimizer]</a> exists and is unique. The function $\\mathcal L$ is lower semicontinuous in each holomorphic coordinate chart, and $$\\label{ell:volume-gap}\n F_0:=\\log\\det b_t-\\mathcal L\n \\quad\\text{satisfies}\\quad 0\\le F_0\\le\\rho.$$ At any fixed point, choose linear cotangent coordinates in which $B=I$. There are finitely many unit columns $\\xi_i$ and positive numbers $\\alpha_i$ such that $$\\label{ell:contact-resolution}\n v(x,\\xi_i,t)=1,\\qquad\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\alpha_i\\xi_i\\xi_i^*=I,\n \\qquad \\sum_{i=1}^m\\alpha_i=n.$$ The largest weight can be made arbitrarily small by repeating columns.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }

    },

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-harnack-completeness",
          "exact_quote": "The flow of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:localized-flow\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:localized-flow\">4</a> has nonnegative holomorphic bisectional curvature and is complete at every positive time. Its Ricci tensor is positive definite. Writing $A=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}(h_t)$ and $R=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}_{h_t}A$, one has, in fixed holomorphic coordinates, $$\\begin{aligned}\n A_t+A h_t^{-1}A+t^{-1}A&\\ge0,\n \\label{chart:matrix-harnack}\\\\\n R_t+t^{-1}R+2\\operatorname{Re}\\partial_XR+A(X,\\overline X)&\\ge0\n \\qquad(X\\in T^{1,0}M).\n \\label{chart:scalar-harnack}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular $h_{t'}\\le h_t$ for $t'\\ge t$, $R(o,t)\\le C$ for $t\\ge0$, and $$\\label{chart:integrated-harnack}\n R(x,t)\\le R(y,t')\\frac{t'}t\n \\exp\\!\\left(\\frac{C\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_{h_t}(x,y)^2}{t'-t}\\right)\n \\qquad(0<t<t').$$ On each fixed compact set, $h_t\\le C_Kt^{-a_K}g$ for $t\\ge1$, with $a_K>0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-chart-local-charts",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed compact $K\\subset M$, there are $r_K,c_K,C_K>0$ such that, for sufficiently large $t$ and every $p\\in K$, there is a holomorphic local biholomorphism $$\\Phi:B_{\\mathbb C^n}(r_K)\\mathrel{%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F6}%\n  \\longrightarrow%\n  \\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}%\n}M,\\qquad\n \\Phi(0)=p,\\qquad d\\Phi(0)\\text{ unitary for }h_t,$$ with $c_Kg_{\\rm eucl}\\le\\Phi^*h_t\\le C_Kg_{\\rm eucl}$. The pullback metrics have bounds of every fixed derivative order on smaller balls. The corresponding local smooth compactness statement holds for rescaled flows whenever curvature is uniformly bounded on fixed-radius moving balls during a fixed two-sided time interval.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-chart-local-charts"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-static-distortion",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $r,c>0$ and a compact set $K\\subset M$. Suppose $\\Phi:B_{\\mathbb C^n}(r)\\to M$ is a holomorphic local biholomorphism, $\\Phi(0)\\in K$, and $\\widetilde g=\\Phi^*g\\ge c g_{\\rm eucl}$. Let $A$ and $B$ be the least and greatest singular lengths of $d\\Phi(0)$, using the Euclidean metric in the domain and $g$ in the target. There are constants depending only on $K,r,c$ and the initial geometry on a compact neighborhood of $K$ such that $$\\label{chart:static-distortion}\n B\\le C(1+A)^C.$$ In particular these constants are independent of the particular chart and its total distortion.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-ricci-windows",
          "exact_quote": "The function $q$ is nondecreasing, $q\\ge c>0$ for large $s$, and $$\\label{chart:q-growth}\n s\\mathrel{%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=27F6}%\n  \\longrightarrow%\n  \\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}%\n}\\infty,\\qquad\n q\\le Ce^\\tau,\\qquad\n \\liminf_{s\\to\\infty}\\frac{q(s)}s\\le2.$$ Uniformly on every fixed compact set, $\\rho(x,t)/s\\to1$, and there is $c_K>0$ such that, eventually on that compact set, $$\\label{chart:metric-decay}\n e^{-2s}g\\le h_t\\le e^{-c_Ks}g.$$ There are fixed $a_0\\in(0,1)$ and $b_0>0$ such that the least eigenvalue at $o$ of $t\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}(h_t)$ relative to $h_t$ satisfies $$\\label{chart:ricci-lower}\n \\lambda_{\\min}(s)\\ge b_0q(a_0s)\n \\quad\\text{for all sufficiently large }s.$$ Moreover there are $S_i\\to\\infty$ and $K_0<\\infty$ with $$\\label{chart:good-windows}\n q(2S_i)\\le K_0q(a_0S_i).$$ One can choose $s_i\\in[5S_i/4,7S_i/4]$ so that $(\\log q)'(s_i)\\to0$. If $t_i$ corresponds to $s_i$ and $R_i=R(o,t_i)$, the rescaled flows $$\\label{chart:rescaled-flows}\n g_i(z)=R_i h_{t_i+z/R_i}$$ have uniform curvature and derivative bounds on each fixed-radius moving ball about $o$ in a fixed small two-sided time interval. At $(o,0)$ their scalar curvature is one and their Ricci eigenvalues have a fixed positive lower bound.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-shrinking-charts",
          "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large volume clock $s$, there is a holomorphic local biholomorphism $\\Phi_s:B_{\\mathbb C^n}(r_0)\\to M$ centered at $o$, with unitary center derivative for the metric at clock $s$, and $$\\label{chart:uniform-chart-metric}\n C^{-1}g_{\\rm eucl}\\le\\Phi_s^*h_{t(s)}\\le Cg_{\\rm eucl}.$$ For fixed $0<r_1<r_0$, chosen sufficiently small, the transition germs $F_{s,u}=\\Phi_u^{-1}\\Phi_s$, $u\\ge s$, extend to nonsingular holomorphic maps on $B(r_1)$ with a common bound. They fix $0$, compose as germs, and satisfy $$\\label{chart:center-transitions}\n \\|F_{s,u}'(0)\\|\\le1,\\qquad\n |\\det F_{s,u}'(0)|=e^{-(u-s)/2},\\qquad\n \\|F_{s,u}'(0)^{-1}\\|\\le e^{(u-s)/2}.$$ There are coherent inverse branches $f_s$ on neighborhoods of any fixed compact exhaustion set for all sufficiently large $s$, satisfying $f_s(o)=0$ and $\\Phi_sf_s=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Id}}$. A constant $\\sigma>0$, common to all compact sets, satisfies $$\\label{chart:common-exponent}\n \\sup_K|f_s|\\le e^{-\\sigma s}\n \\quad\\text{for every fixed compact }K\n \\text{ and all sufficiently large }s.$$ For every fixed jet order $d$ there is $C_d$ such that $$\\label{chart:transition-jets}\n |J_dF_{s,u}|\\le C_d\\min\\{1,e^{C_ds-\\sigma u}\\}\n \\qquad(u\\ge s\\text{ sufficiently large}).$$ There are $c,\\gamma_0>0$ such that, for each fixed $0<\\gamma\\le\\gamma_0$, one has $$\\label{chart:transition-small-balls}\n \\sup_{|z|\\le e^{-\\gamma s}}|F_{s,u}(z)|\n \\le e^{-c\\gamma u}\n \\qquad\\text{for every }u\\ge s\\ge s_\\gamma.$$ The constants in the last two estimates are independent of the later endpoint $u$; the starting threshold in <a href=\"#chart:common-exponent\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"chart:common-exponent\">[chart:common-exponent]</a> may depend on $K$.",

        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-linear-alignment",
          "exact_quote": "On every run of regular steps there are unipotent matrices $L_k^+$, with off-diagonal entries only in rows of smaller weight than their columns, such that, on putting $L_k^-=D_k^{-1}L_k^+D_k$, the diagonal step derivative remains $D_k$ and every regular jump becomes $$J_k=L_{k+1}^-U_k(L_k^+)^{-1},\\qquad\n (J_k)_{il}=0\\quad\\hbox{if }w_{k+1,i}<w_{k,l}.\n \\label{eq:dyn:aligned-jump}$$ The matrices $L_k^+$ and their inverses have polynomial bounds in the macro index $j$, while $$\\lVert L_k^- -I\\rVert\n \\le j^C\\exp\\left(-c\\frac{\\sqrt{S_j}}{j^2}\\right).\n \\label{eq:dyn:small-start-change}$$ The estimates are uniform in finite terminal horizons, and hold on an infinite regular run by a coefficientwise limit.",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-degree-control",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-polynomial-models",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-polynomial-models",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $d\\ge2$ and a macro width $0<h<1$. After starting at a sufficiently late macro endpoint, there is a grid $s_k\\to\\infty$ with macro endpoints $S_j=e^{hj}$ and the following properties. Each macro interval is either one bad step or is divided into regular steps of length comparable to $\\sqrt{S_j}$. Thus, eventually, $$c_h\\sqrt{s_k}\\le\\Delta s_k:=s_{k+1}-s_k\n \\le(e^h-1)s_k.\n \\label{eq:dyn:grid-size}$$ For $F_k=F_{s_k,s_{k+1}}$ there are polynomial local coordinate changes $H_k$ and polynomial automorphisms $G_k$ of $\\mathbb C^n$ satisfying $$J_d(H_{k+1}\\circ F_k)=J_d(G_k\\circ H_k).\n \\label{eq:dyn:jet-conjugacy}$$ The degrees of $H_k,G_k,G_k^{-1}$ are bounded independently of $k$. The coefficients of $H_k$ and its inverse jet are $\\exp(o(s_k))$; $H_k'(0)$ and its inverse have polynomial bounds in $s_k$. For a constant $C_d$ depending only on the fixed jet order and dimension, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\lVert G_k\\rVert_{\\mathrm{coeff}}+\\lVert G_k^{-1}\\rVert_{\\mathrm{coeff}}\n &\\le \\exp\\bigl(C_d\\Delta s_k+o(s_k)\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:dyn:coefficient-bound}\\\\\n \\lVert G_k'(0)^{-1}\\rVert\n &\\le s_k^{C}\\exp(\\Delta s_k/2).\n \\label{eq:dyn:center-inverse}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, the coefficient $C_d$ of $\\Delta s_k$ is independent of $h$. Finally, for some finite $D$, $$\\deg G_{0,k}\\le D s_k\n \\quad\\text{on an unbounded subsequence of macro endpoints.}\n \\label{eq:dyn:forward-degree}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }

Its image $\\Omega$ is open, and $\\Psi:M\\to\\Omega$ is a biholomorphism. Moreover, on every compact $K\\subset\\Omega$, $$\\sup_K\\lvert G_{0,k}\\rvert\\le e^{-(\\sigma/2)s_k}\n \\quad\\hbox{eventually},\n \\label{eq:unif:forward-smallness}$$ and, at every sufficiently large $k$, $$G_{0,k}^{-1}\\bigl(B(e^{-\\gamma's_k})\\bigr)\\subset\\Omega.\n \\label{eq:unif:inverse-ball-inclusion}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-338.1"
        }

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-uniformization-scope**: A connected noncompact complex manifold admitting a smooth complete Kähler metric with strictly positive holomorphic bisectional curvature is biholomorphic to complex Euclidean space.
- **invariant-nonlinear-scalar-comparison**: The nonlinear scalar comparison forces an upper semicontinuous subsolution to vanish when it is locally bounded and lies between zero and the logarithmic volume loss.
- **invariant-joint-positivity**: The evolving squared cotangent norm is jointly plurisubharmonic in space and complexified logarithmic time, and its disc envelope equals the norm.
- **invariant-compact-chart-contraction**: Coherent inverse chart branches contract on every fixed compact set with a common exponent, while the starting threshold may depend on that compact set.
- **invariant-forward-degree-subsequence**: The degrees of the forward polynomial compositions have a bound linear in the volume clock along an unbounded subsequence of macro endpoints.
- **invariant-global-image-control**: The corrected coordinate limit gives an injective holomorphic map onto an open image, with forward smallness on image compacts and an inverse-ball inclusion.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-weaken-main-hypotheses**: Apply the uniformization conclusion to an incomplete metric, a compact manifold, or curvature without strict holomorphic bisectional positivity. -> The input lies outside the theorem's stated geometric regime.
- **anti-use-proper-interpolation**: Treat the global injective immersion or the strictly plurisubharmonic weight as a proper exhaustion. -> Point separation and strict plurisubharmonicity do not supply the properness needed to confine escaping images.
- **anti-use-unrestricted-scalar-subsolutions**: Conclude that every viscosity subsolution of the nonlinear scalar inequality vanishes. -> The comparison conclusion is unsupported when the subsolution is negative, exceeds the logarithmic volume loss, or lacks the stated local bounds.
- **anti-use-area-independent-disc-compact**: Let the area penalty vanish while keeping a common compact containing all disc interiors. -> Interior compactness at a fixed penalty does not establish compactness independent of that penalty.
- **anti-use-global-injective-local-charts**: Treat each uniform immersed chart as globally injective and continue its inverse without checking monodromy. -> A local biholomorphism may have several sheets, so its inverse germ need not already define a coherent inverse over a compact set.
- **anti-use-all-time-forward-degree**: Replace the subsequential forward-degree estimate by an estimate at every clock or by a bound for inverse composition degrees. -> The stronger degree control is not supplied by the polynomial-model proposition and is unnecessary for the surjectivity argument.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The global uniformization result is an analytic existence theorem whose coordinate map is defined by an infinite limit on an exhaustion, rather than a finite computational construction. The flow construction explicitly states: “A diagonal subsequence over compacts and increasing horizons converges smoothly to $U,P,h$ with $h_0=g$.” The corrected-coordinate lemma likewise requires locally uniform limits of backward polynomial iterations. Finite algebraic checks of individual shears or Schur complements would not certify these analytic limits, completeness, or surjectivity of the global coordinate map.

Each macro interval is either one bad step or is divided into regular steps of length comparable to $\\sqrt{S_j}$. Thus, eventually, $$c_h\\sqrt{s_k}\\le\\Delta s_k:=s_{k+1}-s_k\n \\le(e^h-1)s_k.\n \\label{eq:dyn:grid-size}$$ For $F_k=F_{s_k,s_{k+1}}$ there are polynomial local coordinate changes $H_k$ and polynomial automorphisms $G_k$ of $\\mathbb C^n$ satisfying $$J_d(H_{k+1}\\circ F_k)=J_d(G_k\\circ H_k).\n \\label{eq:dyn:jet-conjugacy}$$ The degrees of $H_k,G_k,G_k^{-1}$ are bounded independently of $k$. The coefficients of $H_k$ and its inverse jet are $\\exp(o(s_k))$; $H_k'(0)$ and its inverse have polynomial bounds in $s_k$. For a constant $C_d$ depending only on the fixed jet order and dimension, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\lVert G_k\\rVert_{\\mathrm{coeff}}+\\lVert G_k^{-1}\\rVert_{\\mathrm{coeff}}\n &\\le \\exp\\bigl(C_d\\Delta s_k+o(s_k)\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:dyn:coefficient-bound}\\\\\n \\lVert G_k'(0)^{-1}\\rVert\n &\\le s_k^{C}\\exp(\\Delta s_k/2).\n \\label{eq:dyn:center-inverse}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, the coefficient $C_d$ of $\\Delta s_k$ is independent of $h$. Finally, for some finite $D$, $$\\deg G_{0,k}\\le D s_k\n \\quad\\text{on an unbounded subsequence of macro endpoints.}\n \\label{eq:dyn:forward-degree}$$",
            "source_object": "stmt-prop-polynomial-models"
          },
          {
            "anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-global-coordinates-2",
            "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-prop-global-coordinates</summary>\n\n*Proof of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:uniformization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:uniformization\">1</a>.* The preceding analytic and chart constructions, followed by Propositions <a href=\"#prop:polynomial-models\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:polynomial-models\">27</a> and <a href=\"#prop:global-coordinates\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:global-coordinates\">32</a>, give a biholomorphism $\\Psi:M\\to\\Omega$ onto an open set containing zero, with the forward smallness and inverse-ball inclusion proved above.",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-main-geometric-hypotheses** (hypothesis): Uniformization requires connectedness, noncompactness, and a smooth complete Kähler metric with strictly positive holomorphic bisectional curvature. Blocked use: ['Apply the conclusion to compact manifolds, incomplete metrics, or general Hermitian manifolds.', 'Replace strict bisectional positivity by nonnegativity without an additional theorem.']
- **limit-complex-structure-not-metric-flatness** (formal_scope): The conclusion identifies the complex structure and does not assert that the supplied metric is Euclidean. Blocked use: ['Infer an isometry to Euclidean space or vanishing curvature of the initial metric.']
- **limit-pointwise-curvature-and-local-constants** (hypothesis): Strict positivity is pointwise, and curvature constants extracted on compact sets need not extend uniformly over the manifold. Blocked use: ['Assume a uniform positive curvature lower bound from the main hypothesis.', 'Reuse compact curvature constants globally.']
- **limit-initial-weights-not-proper** (formal_scope): The injective holomorphic immersion and strictly plurisubharmonic function are not asserted to be proper. Blocked use: ['Treat either object as a proper exhaustion.', 'Use the immersion alone to prevent disc images from escaping.']
- **limit-fixed-center-average-estimates** (hypothesis): Initial distance, volume, and averaged curvature estimates use the fixed initial metric and may depend on the chosen center. Blocked use: ['Claim center-independent averaged curvature bounds.', 'Substitute evolving metric balls into initial-metric estimates without justification.']
- **limit-hodge-heat-data-classes** (hypothesis): The order-preserving heat construction uses bounded real forms; the scalar potential identity additionally requires smooth closed data, and Hessian preservation has its own Lipschitz and support assumptions. Blocked use: ['Apply the closed-form potential identity to arbitrary nonclosed forms.', 'Apply the smoothing bound to unrestricted functions without the stated current Hessian and support conditions.']
- **limit-flow-completeness-proof-order** (formal_scope): The localized flow is constructed before positive-time completeness is proved. Its early scalar comparisons cannot rely on completeness of the evolving slices. Blocked use: ['Invoke a complete-flow maximum principle during the localized construction without establishing its hypotheses.', 'Use positive-time completeness as an input to the scalar comparison that precedes it.']
- **limit-nonlinear-comparison-order-interval** (hypothesis): Nonlinear scalar vanishing requires an upper semicontinuous function bounded on compact spacetime sets, lying between zero and the logarithmic volume loss, and satisfying the specified upper viscosity inequality. Blocked use: ['Conclude vanishing for negative subsolutions or subsolutions exceeding the logarithmic volume loss.', 'Drop local boundedness or the viscosity inequality.']
- **limit-disc-compactness-needs-area** (hypothesis): Compact boundary control must be accompanied by the area bound. For cotangent discs the bound concerns projected area, and the resulting compact may depend on both the boundary compact and the area allowance. Blocked use: ['Claim uniform compact containment from compact boundaries alone.', 'Remove dependence of the containing compact on the area bound.']
- **limit-puncture-exponent-not-uniform** (nonconstructive): Extra integrability requires the curvature, bounded injective bundle-map, and integrability hypotheses. Its positive exponent may depend on the section and bundle data. Blocked use: ['Assign a common extra-integrability exponent to all sections.', 'Apply puncture removal after dropping boundedness or pointwise injectivity of the bundle map.']
- **limit-psh-compactness-collapse** (edge_case): Local upper bounds alone allow collapse to negative infinity; a lower anchor excludes that alternative. Blocked use: ['Assert a noncollapsed plurisubharmonic subsequential limit using only local upper bounds.', 'Infer compact supremum convergence without the constant-limit hypothesis.']
- **limit-boundary-factorization-spectral-gap** (hypothesis): Boundary factorization assumes a smooth Hermitian matrix function with a strictly positive lower spectral bound and finite upper spectral bound on the circle. Blocked use: ['Apply the inverse-factor estimates to singular boundary matrices.', 'Replace the full boundary function by samples and claim the exact analytic factorization.']
- **limit-deformation-constants-and-penalty** (approximation): The penalty coefficient in the deformation estimate depends only on boundary data, but realization radii and Taylor bounds may depend on the interior compact and fixed localization parameters. Blocked use: ['Assume one interior compact persists as the area penalty vanishes.', 'Allow interior-dependent realization constants to multiply the final energy penalty.']
- **limit-positive-vertical-test-block** (hypothesis): The disc-envelope differential inequality requires a lower test with positive vertical Levi block. The Schur comparison also assumes a positive majorant and a positive vertical block of the lower form. Blocked use: ['Invert a singular vertical block in the Schur-complement argument.', 'Apply the viscosity inequality to arbitrary lower tests without vertical positivity.']
- **limit-fiber-optimizer-universal-constraint** (nonconstructive): The fiber optimizer is constrained by the envelope in every fiber direction. Its existence and contact resolution do not supply a finite procedure for evaluating the analytic envelope. Blocked use: ['Replace the universal feasibility condition by finitely sampled directions and retain the exact optimizer.', 'Infer a certified optimization stopping rule from existence and uniqueness.']
- **limit-immersed-charts-and-monodromy** (formal_scope): Uniform charts are local biholomorphisms and may have several sheets. Coherent inverse branches require the later continuation and simple-connectivity argument. Blocked use: ['Treat the immersed charts as globally injective.', 'Continue inverse germs over compact sets without controlling monodromy.']
- **limit-compact-contraction-thresholds** (formal_scope): The inverse-chart contraction exponent is common to fixed compact sets, but the starting threshold may depend on the compact set. This is not uniform contraction over the entire noncompact manifold. Blocked use: ['Use a compact-independent starting clock everywhere on the manifold.', 'Promote convergence on each fixed compact to a global supremum estimate.']
- **limit-clock-growth-only-subsequential** (formal_scope): The clock-speed growth information used for degree control provides a selected subsequence rather than an all-time linear ratio bound. Blocked use: ['Assume the clock speed is bounded linearly at every sufficiently late clock.', 'Replace selected Ricci windows by uniform pinching at every time.']
- **limit-finite-jet-realization-scope** (hypothesis): Polynomial automorphism realization applies to invertible constant-volume jets fixing zero. The correcting shears preserve determinant, while the full automorphism retains the determinant of its original linear map. Blocked use: ['Realize an arbitrary invertible jet without checking the constant-volume condition.', 'Assert that every full polynomial model has determinant one.', 'Treat degree and coefficient bounds as a stated runtime bound.']
- **limit-fixed-parameter-coefficient-bounds** (formal_scope): Subexponential coefficient bounds are taken after fixing the construction parameters; their rate is not asserted uniform as those parameters vary. Blocked use: ['Choose the macro width using a constant whose dependence on later choices has not been controlled.', 'Exchange parameter choices and late-start limits without the stated uniformities.']
- **limit-backward-iteration-valid-tubes** (approximation): The corrected-coordinate limit is proved for the specified raw sequences and sufficiently large indices. Inverse Lipschitz estimates are used only inside their stated balls. Blocked use: ['Apply the inverse estimate to arbitrary orbits outside the controlled tubes.', 'Interpret the asymptotic shadow estimate as an unconditional global stability bound.']
- **limit-surjectivity-needs-forward-degree** (formal_scope): An injective holomorphic coordinate map onto an open image does not alone prove surjectivity. The final argument additionally uses forward smallness, inverse-ball inclusion, and the available subsequential forward-degree bound. Blocked use: ['Conclude that shrinking charts alone have image equal to all complex Euclidean space.', 'Replace the forward-composition degree estimate by an unsupported inverse-degree estimate.']
- **limit-global-coordinate-infinite-limit** (nonconstructive): Global coordinates are defined by a locally uniform infinite limit on an exhaustion. The source does not provide a finite stopping certificate or computational complexity for producing the global biholomorphism from general manifold data. Blocked use: ['Advertise a terminating general coordinate solver.', 'Treat finite algebraic checks of jets or matrices as certification of the global analytic uniformization.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every complete connected noncompact Kähler manifold with strictly positive holomorphic bisectional curvature is biholomorphic to complex Euclidean space. This resolves Yau's uniformization conjecture positively in every complex dimension.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

| [stmt-flow-cutoff-lemma](#stmt-flow-cutoff-lemma) | lemma | `flow:cutoff-lemma` | 5 | For every sufficiently large fixed integer $k$, there is a positive function $\chi(x,t)$ on $M\times[0,T]$, smooth in space and continuously differentiable in time one-sided at the initial surface, su |
| [stmt-prop-scalar-comparison](#stmt-prop-scalar-comparison) | proposition | `prop:scalar-comparison` | 6 | Fix a finite $T>0$. The following conclusions hold for the flow of Theorem 4.* |

| [stmt-lem-symplectic-evolution](#stmt-lem-symplectic-evolution) | lemma | `lem:symplectic-evolution` | 8 | For the flow of Theorem 4, |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

In his 1982 problem survey, Yau asked whether a complete noncompact Kähler manifold with positive holomorphic bisectional curvature must be biholomorphic to complex Euclidean space <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[YauProblems](#ref-YauProblems), 9(b), p. 45]. We prove the conjecture under its pointwise positivity hypothesis.

<a id="stmt-thm-uniformization"></a>
<div id="thm:uniformization" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $M$ be a connected noncompact complex manifold of complex dimension $n\geq1$. If $M$ admits a smooth complete Kähler metric with strictly positive holomorphic bisectional curvature, then $M$ is biholomorphic to $\mathbb C^n$.*

</div>

Strict positivity in Theorem <a href="#thm:uniformization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:uniformization">1</a> is pointwise. In particular, the theorem assumes neither a uniform positive lower bound nor a finite upper bound for the curvature. No volume-growth, noncollapsing, Ricci-pinching, or topological hypothesis is added. Its conclusion identifies the entire complex manifold with $\mathbb C^n$; it does not assert that the given metric is Euclidean. In particular, $M$ is Stein and contractible. It resolves Yau’s uniformization conjecture for strictly positive holomorphic bisectional curvature in every complex dimension.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Antecedents and the remaining analytic difficulties

The one-dimensional case belongs to the classical relation between curvature and conformal type. A complete noncompact Riemann surface of positive Gaussian curvature is conformally the plane, by Cohn-Vossen’s topology theorem and the parabolicity theorem of Blanc–Fiala–Huber <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[CohnVossen](#ref-CohnVossen), Satz 8]<a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Huber](#ref-Huber), Theorem 15]. In the compact setting, the Frankel conjecture was resolved by Mori and by Siu–Yau: positive holomorphic bisectional curvature characterizes complex projective space <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[MoriAmple](#ref-MoriAmple), [SiuYauFrankel](#ref-SiuYauFrankel)]. Yau’s noncompact question asks for an analogous characterization of complex Euclidean space while allowing unrestricted geometry at infinity <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[YauProblems](#ref-YauProblems), 9(b), p. 45].

One route to this problem studies holomorphic functions and algebraic embeddings. Greene–Wu showed that positive real sectional curvature yields a smooth strictly convex exhaustion and hence Steinness <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[GreeneWuConvex1976](#ref-GreeneWuConvex1976), Theorem 10]. Mok obtained an affine-algebraic embedding under positive bisectional curvature, maximal volume growth, and quadratic scalar-curvature decay <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[MokEmbedding1984](#ref-MokEmbedding1984)]. These results connect curvature to global function theory while retaining hypotheses or conclusions different from unrestricted uniformization. Chen–Zhu proved intrinsic growth and average-curvature estimates under pointwise positive bisectional curvature <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[ChenZhu](#ref-ChenZhu)]. In particular, their linear average-scalar-curvature bound requires no upper curvature bound, but its constant may depend on the center. They also pursued algebraic compactification under bounded positive sectional curvature and a finite top Ricci integral <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[ChenZhuPositive2009](#ref-ChenZhuPositive2009)]. Ni–Tam developed heat deformation of plurisubharmonic functions under nonnegative bisectional curvature, without a global upper curvature bound <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[NiTamStructure](#ref-NiTamStructure)]. Their smoothing theorem keeps an explicit growth condition on the initial function.

A second route uses geometric flow to construct shrinking holomorphic charts and then assembles those charts by complex dynamics. Cao’s differential Harnack estimates for Kähler–Ricci flow <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[CaoHarnack](#ref-CaoHarnack)] are an analytic antecedent of this approach. Chau–Tam established uniformization under bounded nonnegative bisectional curvature and maximal volume growth <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[ChauTamComplex2006](#ref-ChauTamComplex2006)]. Their later work developed locally biholomorphic charts, covering and pseudoconvex-domain conclusions, and soliton-limit methods under additional curvature-decay or pinching conditions <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[ChauTamStein](#ref-ChauTamStein), [ChauTamSimply](#ref-ChauTamSimply)]. The distinction between a covering, a map onto a domain, and a biholomorphism onto all of $\mathbb C^n$ is essential here. Liu proved uniformization under nonnegative bisectional curvature and maximal volume growth, without an upper curvature bound, using a Gromov–Hausdorff approach and retracting holomorphic vector fields <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[LiuUniformization](#ref-LiuUniformization)]. Lee–Tam subsequently obtained the same conclusion under these hypotheses by smoothing the metric and applying the bounded-curvature theorem <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[LeeTam](#ref-LeeTam)]. Both results retain maximal volume growth.

Recent work has removed assumptions at infinity in important special cases.[^1] Datar, Pingali, and Seshadri <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[DatarPingaliSeshadri](#ref-DatarPingaliSeshadri), Theorem 1] prove that every complete noncompact Kähler surface with positive real sectional curvature is biholomorphic to $\mathbb C^2$. Their method uses Lipschitz plurisubharmonic weights of finite Monge–Ampère mass and weighted holomorphic functions. For positive bisectional curvature, their Theorem 2 gives the same conclusion under strong Steinness, contractibility, and simple connectivity at infinity. Here strong Steinness means the existence of a smooth plurisubharmonic exhaustion with bounded gradient. Wu <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[WuConditional](#ref-WuConditional), Corollaries 1.5–1.6] removes contractibility from this criterion: strong Steinness and simple connectivity at infinity suffice. Wu also obtains the conclusion under positive bisectional curvature when the latter topological condition holds and the real sectional curvatures are nonnegative outside a compact set. These are surface results; positive real sectional curvature is a stronger hypothesis than positive holomorphic bisectional curvature.

The finite-mass approach also leads to higher-dimensional results. Datar, Pingali, and Seshadri <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[DatarPingaliSeshadriTop](#ref-DatarPingaliSeshadriTop), Theorem 1.1] prove finiteness of the integral of the top power of the Ricci form on complete noncompact Kähler manifolds with positive real sectional curvature. Their theorem also applies under positive bisectional curvature when a smooth strictly plurisubharmonic exhaustion with uniformly bounded gradient is available; with positive bounded sectional curvature, they obtain quasiprojectivity <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[DatarPingaliSeshadriTop](#ref-DatarPingaliSeshadriTop), Theorem 1.2]. Dat <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[DatHessian](#ref-DatHessian)] develops an $m$-Hessian capacity approach to finite-Monge–Ampère weights in complex dimensions at least three; its proposed uniformization route retains proper-map and multiplicity assumptions.

A complementary direction relates holomorphic growth to the asymptotic geometry of the Kähler–Ricci flow. Shi <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[ShiMinimalDegrees](#ref-ShiMinimalDegrees), Theorem 1.6] proves an identity between the minimal growth degree of holomorphic volume forms and the refined minimal degrees of holomorphic functions for complete noncompact Kähler manifolds with nonnegative bisectional curvature and nonsplitting universal cover. Under maximal volume growth, the flow results and coordinates in <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[ShiMinimalDegrees](#ref-ShiMinimalDegrees), Theorem 1.7 and Corollary 1.12] connect these holomorphic degrees with Lyapunov exponents. This gives a quantitative connection between the function-theoretic and flow approaches to uniformization in that growth regime.

Two issues must be addressed in the unrestricted problem. First, the initial metric can have unbounded curvature and arbitrarily collapsed regions. A complete bounded-curvature flow theorem or a global tensor maximum principle cannot therefore be used without first establishing its hypotheses. Second, shrinking charts alone can identify the manifold with a proper domain in $\mathbb C^n$. To obtain surjectivity, one needs quantitative control of their coordinate changes even when contraction rates vary substantially.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Structure of the proof

Write $g$ for the initial metric and fix $o\in M$. We construct a global Kähler–Ricci flow $h_t$ by complete Hermitian approximations on exhausting domains. Estimates for the metric and its logarithmic volume loss $\rho(x,t)=\log(\det g(x)/\det h_t(x))$ pass to the limit without global curvature control. A spatial weight adapted to the nonnegative form $g-h_t$ then proves scalar comparison before completeness of $h_t$ is known. This order is important: completeness is a later consequence of the Harnack inequality.

The curvature argument takes place on the holomorphic cotangent bundle. We minimize the boundary squared dual norm over holomorphic discs, with compact boundary walls and a vanishing area penalty. The canonical symplectic bivector relates the complex Hessian of the norm to its time derivative. A quantitative deformation lemma gives a viscosity inequality for the disc infimum; optimizing a Hermitian ellipsoid in each fiber converts that inequality into the scalar comparison already proved. The infimum therefore equals the evolving squared dual norm. This gives joint positivity in space and logarithmic time, hence the matrix and scalar Harnack inequalities.

The proof uses three time parameters, each for a different estimate:

<div class="center">

| Clock            | Definition    | Role                                     |
|:-----------------|:--------------|:-----------------------------------------|
| Physical time    | $t$           | Kähler–Ricci flow                        |
| Logarithmic time | $\tau=\log t$ | Joint positivity and Harnack estimates   |
| Volume clock     | $s=\rho(o,t)$ | Chart contraction and polynomial degrees |

</div>

The volume clock becomes strictly increasing by the Ricci positivity proved in Section <a href="#sec:charts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:charts">7</a>. Sections <a href="#sec:discs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:discs">4</a>–<a href="#sec:variation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:variation">5</a> use $s$ temporarily for the physical time at a disc center; from Section <a href="#sec:charts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:charts">7</a> onward it always denotes the volume clock.

The volume clock measures contraction directly. For the transition $F_{s,u}$ from a centered unitary chart at clock $s$ to one at $u\ge s$, $$|\det F_{s,u}'(0)|=e^{-(u-s)/2}.$$ Writing $R(x,t)$ for the scalar curvature of $h_t$, the speed of the volume clock in logarithmic time is $q(s)=ds/d\tau=tR(o,t)$. A static logarithmic-norm estimate bounds the greatest singular length of an $h_t$-unitary local chart, measured in the initial metric $g$, by a fixed power of its least singular length. Together with the determinant loss, this controls contraction in every direction. Selected time intervals with Ricci pinching then give a contraction argument for loops. Simple connectivity allows the local inverse charts to continue coherently over exhausting compact sets, and a plurisubharmonic Liouville argument gives one contraction exponent valid on every fixed compact set.

Finally, we normalize the volume jets of the transition maps and construct polynomial automorphisms with constant Jacobian. The nonlinear shears used to realize normalized jets have determinant one; the full models retain their contracting linear determinant. Intervals of nonuniform contraction are allowed: their degree cost is charged to growth of $q$ in the volume clock. Inverse iteration of these models makes the corrected inverse charts converge to an injective holomorphic map $M\to\mathbb C^n$. A subsequential bound for the degree of the forward compositions, together with smallness on compact subsets of the image and an inverse-ball inclusion, rules out a finite maximal radius for centered balls in the image. This proves that the limiting coordinate map is onto all of $\mathbb C^n$.

These mechanisms are proved in the order in which they are used. Sections <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a>–<a href="#sec:flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:flow">3</a> establish the initial analytic data and scalar comparison. Sections <a href="#sec:discs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:discs">4</a>–<a href="#sec:ellipsoids" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:ellipsoids">6</a> prove joint positivity by discs and ellipsoids. Section <a href="#sec:charts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:charts">7</a> obtains completeness, simple connectivity, and quantitative shrinking charts. Sections <a href="#sec:dynamics" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:dynamics">8</a>–<a href="#sec:uniformization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:uniformization">9</a> construct the biholomorphism. In particular, the disc-deformation estimate and the forward-degree estimate are stated with the uniformities needed at their later applications.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Conventions

We use the real curvature convention in which $\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}(a,b,b,a)$ is the sectional-curvature numerator of the plane spanned by $a,b$. The hypothesis is $$\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}(u,v,v,u)+\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}(u,Jv,Jv,u)>0$$ for every pair of real unit tangent vectors $u,v$ at every point. By the Kähler symmetries, this is equivalent to Griffiths strict positivity of $T^{1,0}M$ in complex notation. In complex dimension one the displayed sum equals Gaussian curvature.

We identify a Hermitian tensor $(a_{i\bar j})$ with its real $(1,1)$-form, suppressing the factor $\sqrt{-1}$ when no confusion can result. Thus $\partial\bar\partial f$ denotes the complex Hessian of $f$, $\Delta_h f=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_h\partial\bar\partial f$, and $$\partial_t h_t=-\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(h_t),\qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(h)=-\partial\bar\partial\log\det h.$$ All Laplacian, curvature, and volume conventions in the proof use this normalization. The dual of a Hermitian metric includes the transpose dictated by the holomorphic cotangent pairing; matrix contractions below retain it explicitly when necessary.

The initial complete metric is $g$, a fixed base point is $o$, and $r(x)=d_g(o,x)$. Constants may change between estimates. Subscripts indicate dependencies when those dependencies matter. For Hermitian forms, $A=O(b)h$ means $-Cbh\leq A\leq Cbh$. The abbreviation psh means plurisubharmonic. A positive constant extracted from curvature on a specified compact set is never understood to be uniform over all of $M$.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Functions, sections, and smoothing on the initial manifold

Throughout this Section, distances, balls, norms, and volume are those of the complete initial metric $g$. Fix $o\in M$ and put $r(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_g(o,x)$. Constants may depend on $g$ and $o$. In particular, the estimates below are not estimates uniform over all choices of center.

We prepare the functions and estimates used in the flow and disc arguments.
